# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Body styles
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def make_styles():
    ss = getSampleStyleSheet()
    body = ParagraphStyle('Body', parent=ss['BodyText'],
                           fontName='BodySerif', fontSize=10.5, leading=15.5,
                           alignment=TA_LEFT, textColor=TEXT_PRIMARY,
                           spaceBefore=0, spaceAfter=8)
    h1 = ParagraphStyle('H1', parent=ss['Heading1'],
                          fontName='BodySerifB', fontSize=18, leading=22,
                          textColor=HEADER_FILL, spaceBefore=14, spaceAfter=10,
                          keepWithNext=1)
    h2 = ParagraphStyle('H2', parent=ss['Heading2'],
                          fontName='BodySerifB', fontSize=13, leading=17,
                          textColor=ACCENT, spaceBefore=10, spaceAfter=6,
                          keepWithNext=1)
    h3 = ParagraphStyle('H3', parent=ss['Heading3'],
                          fontName='BodySerifB', fontSize=11, leading=14,
                          textColor=TEXT_PRIMARY, spaceBefore=6, spaceAfter=4,
                          keepWithNext=1)
    mono = ParagraphStyle('Mono', parent=body, fontName='Mono',
                            fontSize=9, leading=12, textColor=TEXT_PRIMARY,
                            leftIndent=8, rightIndent=8,
                            backColor=CARD_BG, borderPadding=4)
    callout = ParagraphStyle('Callout', parent=body,
                               fontName='BodySerifB', fontSize=10.5,
                               textColor=ACCENT_2, leftIndent=10, spaceBefore=4, spaceAfter=10)
    return body, h1, h2, h3, mono, callout


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Build story
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def build_story():
    body, h1, h2, h3, mono, callout = make_styles()
    S = lambda h: Spacer(1, h)
    story = []

    # The cover canvas (_draw_cover) draws on page 1.
    # Push the body to start on page 2 so the executive summary is visible.
    story.append(PageBreak())

    # ---- Title block (top of body, page 2 onward) ----
    story.append(Paragraph("1.  Executive Summary", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "This report documents a CPU-only computational experiment that tests two "
        "core hypotheses from the attached ARC notes against the actual GLM "
        "substrate at <font color='#4e769d'>github.com/DigitalEuan/GLM</font>. "
        "The user-confirmed scope was: (a) Note 5's hypothesis that a Leech-lattice "
        "representation improves the GLM's ability to infer or execute geometric "
        "operators, and (b) Notes 1-2's hypothesis that teaching verified "
        "transformation traces (rather than only final answers) improves a binary "
        "recurrent GLM's ability to solve geometric tasks. As a sanity check we "
        "also re-ran the Note 4 math audit chain (F<sub>2</sub> -> Hamming -> Golay "
        "G<sub>23</sub>/G<sub>24</sub> -> Leech &#923;<sub>24</sub> -> Conway) "
        "against the substrate's own self-checks.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Three concrete experiments were run: (A) a solver-comparison on 5 "
        "synthetic binary task families where the displacement &#916;<sub>x</sub> "
        "either transfers (toggle) or does not (translate, reflect_v, flood_fill, "
        "bfs_shortest), with four solvers (oracle, raw XOR, Golay-coset, random "
        "linear); (C) the Note 5 disambiguation test that contrasts the "
        "displacement hypothesis against operator-inferred rules; and (B) a "
        "tiny binary recurrent GLM with the Note 1 interface "
        "b<sub>t+1</sub> = F<sub>&#952;</sub>(b<sub>t</sub>, z<sub>D</sub>), trained "
        "with and without trace supervision on the same five task families.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("Headline findings (statistically clean):", h3))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "1. <b>Note 4 math audit:</b> 22/22 substrate self-checks PASS. The chain "
        "F<sub>2</sub> -> Hamming -> G<sub>23</sub>/G<sub>24</sub> -> "
        "&#923;<sub>24</sub> -> Co<sub>0</sub>/Co<sub>1</sub> is real, exact, and "
        "matches every cited theorem (sphere-packing bound, Steiner S(5,8,24), "
        "Construction A/B/C ladder, theta series E<sub>4</sub><sup>3</sup> &#8722; "
        "720&#916;, no roots).", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "2. <b>Note 5 (Leech representation):</b> the Golay-coset binary "
        "quotient adds <b>no transfer power</b> beyond an arbitrary F<sub>2</sub> "
        "linear projection. Across 250 trials, the Golay-coset solver and a "
        "random 12x24 parity-matrix solver produced identical exact-match "
        "results in <b>249/250 trials</b>. Both fail to recover even the toggle "
        "task (where &#916;<sub>x</sub> is invariant). The specific Golay "
        "structure (Steiner S(5,8,24), octads, MOG geometry) provides no benefit "
        "for transferring geometric operators in this setup.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "3. <b>Notes 1-2 (trace supervision):</b> trace supervision reliably "
        "improves intermediate-grid alignment (IMR) on every task family, and "
        "improves final-answer accuracy on the task where final-only training "
        "struggles most (toggle: +0.157 EM, 0.250 vs 0.093). On tasks already "
        "solved by final-only training (translate, flood_fill: 100% EM in both "
        "regimes), trace supervision provides no extra final-EM benefit but "
        "still improves IMR substantially. The hypothesis is partially "
        "supported: trace supervision helps where the task is hard, and always "
        "helps intermediate state alignment.", body))
    story.append(S(6))

    # ---- Section 2: Note 4 Math Audit ----
    story.append(Paragraph("2.  Note 4 Math Audit", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Note 4 claims a deterministic chain: starting from a binary toggle "
        "{0, 1} with the involution &#964;(x) = 1 &#8853; x, and imposing the "
        "extremal axiom of perfect binary error correction, the chain "
        "F<sub>2</sub> -> Hamming -> G<sub>23</sub>/G<sub>24</sub> -> "
        "&#923;<sub>24</sub> -> Co<sub>0</sub>/Co<sub>1</sub> unfolds without "
        "any further choices, with no random seeds, no floats, and no "
        "approximations. The GLM substrate implements every link of this chain "
        "in pure Python with exact integer arithmetic, so the audit reduces to "
        "invoking the substrate's own self-checks.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "The audit script (<font name='Mono' size='9'>scripts/01_audit_note4.py</font>) "
        "runs 22 self-checks covering: F<sub>2</sub> toggle algebra, the Hamming "
        "metric via XOR popcount, F<sub>2</sub>-rank dimension 24, the sphere-"
        "packing bound &#931;<sub>k=0..3</sub> C(23,k) = 2<sup>11</sup>, the "
        "extended Golay code parameters [24,12,8] with 4,096 codewords, the "
        "weight enumerator 1 + 759z<sup>8</sup> + 2576z<sup>12</sup> + "
        "759z<sup>16</sup> + z<sup>24</sup>, doubly-even parity, the 759 octads "
        "as the Steiner system S(5,8,24), puncturing G<sub>24</sub> to obtain "
        "G<sub>23</sub> = [23,12,7], the Construction A/B/C ladder (48 -> "
        "98,256 -> 196,560), the necessity report (dropping any one condition "
        "breaks the packing), the index [Z<sup>24</sup> : &#923;<sub>24</sub>] = "
        "2<sup>36</sup>, the no-roots condition (min norm 4, not 2), the theta "
        "series E<sub>4</sub><sup>3</sup> &#8722; 720&#916;, the Co<sub>0</sub> "
        "pair-census {4:2, 2:9200, 1:94208, 0:93150}, the 98,280 type-2 axes, "
        "and the full class census 1 + 98,280 + 8,386,560 + 8,292,375 = "
        "2<sup>24</sup>.", body))

    # Audit chain figure
    story.append(S(6))
    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/audit_chain.png")
    img.drawWidth = 16.5*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (1/3.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 1.</i> The full Note 4 chain, with substrate-verified facts "
        "below each box. Every arrow is an exact arithmetic operation; no floats, "
        "no approximations.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>Verdict:</b> 22 of 22 checks PASS. The math audit chain is not "
        "merely asserted; it is recomputed from F<sub>2</sub> by the substrate "
        "itself. The one conceptual correction needed by the note is already "
        "acknowledged in NOTE 4: the chain is not <i>forced</i> by the toggle "
        "alone; the extremal axiom (perfect error correction) must be added "
        "explicitly to select length 23 over the infinitely many other possible "
        "lengths. The substrate's <font name='Mono' size='9'>necessity_report</font> "
        "demonstrates this directly: dropping the mod-4 Golay condition collapses "
        "the minimum to 8 (a 552-vector packing), and dropping the mod-8 sum "
        "condition collapses it to 16 (the 48-vector Construction A lattice). "
        "The Leech lattice is the unique no-root Niemeier lattice in dimension "
        "24; the substrate verifies this by computing min norm 32 in the "
        "x&#8730;8 integer model, strictly greater than the Construction-A "
        "minimum of 16.", body))

    story.append(PageBreak())

    # ---- Section 3: Experiment A+C (Note 5) ----
    story.append(Paragraph("3.  Experiment A+C (Note 5): Leech/Golay representation", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Note 5's strongest falsifiable claim is that a Leech-based representation "
        "<i>improves</i> the GLM's ability to infer or execute geometric operators "
        "on held-out puzzles. To test this we built five synthetic binary task "
        "families on 4x6 grids (24 bits = one Leech coordinate, no truncation "
        "or hashing required):", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "&#8226; <b>toggle</b> (y = NOT x). The displacement &#916;<sub>x</sub> = "
        "all-ones, which is <i>invariant</i> across all examples. By Note 5's "
        "own criterion, displacement <b>should</b> transfer here.<br/>"
        "&#8226; <b>translate</b> (move a 2x2 block by (dy, dx)). "
        "&#916;<sub>x</sub> depends on the block's position. Displacement should "
        "<b>not</b> transfer.<br/>"
        "&#8226; <b>reflect_v</b> (mirror across vertical axis). "
        "&#916;<sub>x</sub> depends on grid contents. Displacement should "
        "<b>not</b> transfer (except for symmetric inputs).<br/>"
        "&#8226; <b>flood_fill</b> (fill interior of a closed boundary). "
        "&#916;<sub>x</sub> depends on the boundary's geometry. Displacement "
        "should <b>not</b> transfer.<br/>"
        "&#8226; <b>bfs_shortest</b> (mark shortest Manhattan path between two "
        "marked bits). &#916;<sub>x</sub> depends on the two positions. "
        "Displacement should <b>not</b> transfer.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("3.1  Solvers", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Four solvers were compared on each (demo, query) pair: <b>(1) oracle</b> "
        "applies the rule by name (upper bound); <b>(2) raw XOR</b> applies "
        "y<sub>q</sub> := x<sub>q</sub> &#8853; (x<sub>1</sub> &#8853; "
        "y<sub>1</sub>) -- the displacement baseline; <b>(3) Golay-coset</b> "
        "works in the F<sub>2</sub><sup>24</sup> / G<sub>24</sub> quotient: "
        "compute syndromes s(x<sub>1</sub>), s(y<sub>1</sub>), s(x<sub>q</sub>), "
        "transfer the syndrome displacement &#916;<sub>s</sub> = "
        "s(y<sub>1</sub>) &#8853; s(x<sub>1</sub>), decode via the canonical "
        "coset leader, and apply as an XOR to x<sub>q</sub>; <b>(4) random "
        "linear</b> uses the same pipeline but with a random 12x24 parity-check "
        "matrix in place of the Golay code's, to isolate whether the Golay "
        "code's specific structure (Steiner system, octads) adds anything beyond "
        "arbitrary F<sub>2</sub> dimensionality reduction.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("3.2  Results", h2))
    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/exp_AC_bars.png")
    img.drawWidth = 16.5*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (5.5/11.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 2.</i> Exact-match accuracy across 50 trials per task family. "
        "Oracle = upper bound; raw XOR handles only toggle (where &#916; is "
        "invariant); both Golay-coset and random-linear fail everywhere, "
        "including on toggle.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("3.3  Interpretation", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "The headline result is quantitative: across 250 trials the Golay-coset "
        "solver and the random-linear solver produced <b>identical exact-match "
        "results in 249/250 cases</b>. This means the specific Golay code "
        "structure -- including its Steiner system S(5,8,24), the 759 octads, "
        "the Miracle Octad Generator, and the deep automorphism group "
        "M<sub>24</sub> -- provides <b>no transfer power</b> beyond what any "
        "full-rank 12x24 binary matrix would provide. The binary quotient "
        "F<sub>2</sub><sup>24</sup>/G<sub>24</sub> &#8773; F<sub>2</sub><sup>12</sup> "
        "is an abstract F<sub>2</sub>-vector space; the Golay code's structure "
        "is internal to the chosen realization and does not change the linear-"
        "algebraic transfer behavior of the quotient.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "A second finding is that the Golay-coset solver fails even on toggle, "
        "where raw XOR succeeds 100%. The cause is the canonical-coset-leader "
        "decoding step: the syndrome of the all-ones displacement maps to a "
        "specific 12-bit address, but the canonical leader for that address is "
        "the <i>lowest-weight</i> 24-bit word with that syndrome -- which is "
        "typically a low-weight (3- or 4-bit) error pattern, not the all-ones "
        "vector. So even when the syndrome transfers correctly, the decoded "
        "displacement pattern is wrong. This is exactly the Note 5 warning: "
        "<i>a state difference is not generally the rule</i>, and a Leech "
        "codebook does not learn ARC rules.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>Verdict for Note 5:</b> NOT SUPPORTED at the binary-quotient level. "
        "The Leech/Golay binary quotient is information-equivalent to a random "
        "F<sub>2</sub> linear projection for the purpose of transferring "
        "geometric operators from demonstrations to queries. To get any benefit "
        "from the Leech lattice, the experiment would need to go beyond the "
        "F<sub>2</sub> quotient -- e.g., use Leech minimal-vector paths in the "
        "actual 24-dimensional real lattice, with a Euclidean cost function and "
        "tie-breaking rules. That is a substantially different and more "
        "expensive experiment.", callout))

    story.append(PageBreak())

    # ---- Section 4: Experiment B (Notes 1-2) ----
    story.append(Paragraph("4.  Experiment B (Notes 1-2): Trace supervision", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Notes 1 and 2 propose that the GLM should be taught <i>verified "
        "transformation traces</i> -- intermediate grids u<sub>1</sub>, "
        "u<sub>2</sub>, ..., u<sub>k</sub> -- rather than only the final answer "
        "y. The TraceViT paper cited in NOTE 2 finds that this trace supervision "
        "needs supporting task memory and object-workspace structures, but the "
        "core hypothesis is simple: align one GLM macro-step with one teaching "
        "milestone, and minimize BCE at every step, not just the last.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("4.1  Setup", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "We built a tiny binary recurrent GLM following the Note 1 interface: "
        "z<sub>D</sub> = A(D) is the XOR displacement y<sub>1</sub> &#8853; "
        "x<sub>1</sub> (constant for toggle, varies per demo for the other "
        "tasks); b<sub>0</sub> = E(x<sub>q</sub>) places the query grid in "
        "the first 24 of a 32-dimensional state; the recurrent update "
        "b<sub>t+1</sub> = F<sub>&#952;</sub>(b<sub>t</sub>, z<sub>D</sub>) = "
        "tanh(W<sub>t</sub> b<sub>t</sub> + U<sub>t</sub> z + c<sub>t</sub>) is "
        "applied for T=2 steps; a shared readout r<sub>t</sub> = "
        "&#963;(R b<sub>t+1</sub> + d) produces a 24-bit grid at each step. "
        "Training is vanilla SGD with manual backprop-through-time; the loss is "
        "BCE on the readout activations. Two regimes are compared: FINAL_ONLY "
        "(minimize BCE(r<sub>T-1</sub>, y) only) and TRACE (minimize "
        "&#931;<sub>t</sub> BCE(r<sub>t</sub>, u<sub>t</sub>) at every step, "
        "with &#955;<sub>trace</sub>=1.0).", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Crucially, both regimes share the SAME architecture, the SAME data, "
        "the SAME optimizer, and the SAME random seeds -- only the loss differs. "
        "So any accuracy difference is attributable to the trace-supervision "
        "signal itself. We ran 3 seeds x 5 task families x 2 regimes = 30 runs, "
        "with 500 train / 100 val per run and 60 epochs at lr=0.5.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("4.2  Final-answer accuracy", h2))
    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/exp_B_bars.png")
    img.drawWidth = 16.5*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (5.5/10.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 3.</i> Best validation exact-match accuracy (mean &#177; "
        "std across 3 seeds). TRACE helps substantially on toggle (+0.157 EM), "
        "has no effect on the already-solved translate and flood_fill tasks, "
        "and slightly hurts on reflect_v (-0.047) and bfs_shortest (-0.027) -- "
        "the latter two effects are within one standard deviation of noise.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("4.3  Intermediate-grid match rate", h2))
    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/exp_B_imr_bars.png")
    img.drawWidth = 16.5*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (5.5/10.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 4.</i> Best intermediate-grid match rate (IMR) -- the "
        "fraction of cells in the predicted intermediate grids that match the "
        "true intermediate milestone grids. TRACE improves IMR on EVERY task "
        "family, often substantially: toggle 0.70 -> 0.95, translate 0.64 -> "
        "0.79, reflect_v 0.79 -> 0.996, flood_fill 0.86 -> 1.00, bfs_shortest "
        "0.82 -> 0.97.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("4.4  Learning curves", h2))
    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/exp_B_curves.png")
    img.drawWidth = 17*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (4/16.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 5.</i> Validation exact-match accuracy curves (mean &#177; "
        "std across 3 seeds) for each task family. The toggle panel shows the "
        "cleanest TRACE > FINAL_ONLY effect; the translate and flood_fill "
        "panels reach 100% in both regimes; reflect_v and bfs_shortest show "
        "small within-noise differences.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("4.5  Interpretation", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "The trace-supervision hypothesis of Note 2 is <b>partially supported</b>. "
        "On the headline metric -- final-answer exact-match accuracy -- trace "
        "supervision helps where final-only training is weak (toggle, the "
        "hardest task in our set for the tiny GLM, +0.157 EM) and provides no "
        "benefit where final-only already achieves 100% (translate, flood_fill). "
        "On reflect_v and bfs_shortest, trace supervision slightly hurts final "
        "EM, but the effect is within one standard deviation of the noise "
        "(std &#8776; 0.03). The more nuanced finding is on the intermediate-"
        "grid match rate: <b>trace supervision always improves IMR</b>, often "
        "substantially. This is the direct prediction of Note 2: by aligning "
        "the model's intermediate states with the verified milestone grids, "
        "the trace-supervised model learns the right <i>computational path</i>, "
        "not just the right destination. Even when the final answer doesn't "
        "improve, the intermediate states are dramatically more aligned with "
        "the geometric execution trace.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "The toggle result is the cleanest case. The task encoding z = "
        "y<sub>1</sub> &#8853; x<sub>1</sub> is the all-ones vector for every "
        "toggle demonstration, so the model receives a constant task input "
        "and must learn to output NOT x<sub>q</sub>. The FINAL_ONLY regime "
        "struggles because the loss signal only arrives at the final step, "
        "after the recurrence has saturated the state through two tanh "
        "applications. The TRACE regime provides additional gradient signal "
        "at every step, which both speeds up convergence and lands in a "
        "better optimum. This is consistent with the TraceViT finding cited "
        "in NOTE 2 that <i>trace supervision needs supporting task memory and "
        "object-workspace structures; intermediate targets alone are not "
        "enough</i>. In our setup the task memory IS the constant z input, "
        "and the object workspace is the 32-cell state -- both are present, "
        "so the trace signal can land.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>Verdict for Notes 1-2:</b> PARTIALLY SUPPORTED. Trace supervision "
        "reliably improves intermediate-grid alignment (IMR) on every task "
        "family, and improves final-answer accuracy where final-only training "
        "is weak. On tasks already solved by final-only training, it provides "
        "no extra final-EM benefit. The mechanism is exactly the one Note 2 "
        "predicts: the model learns the right computational path, not just "
        "the right destination. The strongest follow-up would be to scale up "
        "the architecture and the task complexity to verify whether the "
        "toggle-style benefit generalises to harder multi-step traces.", callout))

    story.append(PageBreak())

    # ---- Section 5: Verdicts ----
    story.append(Paragraph("5.  Verdicts and next iterations", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph("5.1  Per-note verdicts", h2))

    # Build the verdict table
    verdict_data = [
        ["Note", "Hypothesis", "Verdict", "Evidence"],
        ["Note 4",
         "F_2 -> Hamming -> Golay -> Leech -> Conway chain\n"
         "unfolds deterministically from the binary toggle",
         "VERIFIED\n22/22 PASS",
         "Substrate self-checks: 4096 codewords, 759 octads,\n"
         "S(5,8,24), Construction ladder 48 -> 98,256 -> 196,560,\n"
         "theta series E_4^3 - 720*Delta, [Z^24:Lambda] = 2^36."],
        ["Note 5",
         "Leech-based representation improves the GLM's\n"
         "ability to infer or execute geometric operators",
         "NOT SUPPORTED\nat F_2 quotient",
         "Golay-coset = random-linear in 249/250 trials.\n"
         "Both fail to recover toggle (where Delta_x is\n"
         "invariant). The Golay code's Steiner structure\n"
         "adds no transfer power beyond F_2 linear reduction."],
        ["Notes 1-2",
         "Trace supervision (verified intermediate grids)\n"
         "improves the GLM's ability to solve geometric tasks",
         "PARTIALLY\nSUPPORTED",
         "Toggle: +0.157 EM (TRACE 0.250 vs FINAL 0.093).\n"
         "IMR always improves (toggle 0.70 -> 0.95, etc.).\n"
         "Tasks already solved by FINAL_ONLY (translate,\n"
         "flood_fill) see no extra final-EM benefit."],
    ]
    # Wrap text in Paragraphs for proper line wrapping
    cell_style = ParagraphStyle('Cell', fontName='BodySerif', fontSize=8.5,
                                  leading=11, textColor=TEXT_PRIMARY)
    cell_head  = ParagraphStyle('CellHead', fontName='BodySerifB', fontSize=9,
                                  leading=11, textColor=colors.white,
                                  alignment=TA_CENTER)
    cell_verd  = ParagraphStyle('CellVerd', fontName='BodySerifB', fontSize=9,
                                  leading=11, textColor=TEXT_PRIMARY,
                                  alignment=TA_CENTER)
    wrapped = []
    for i, row in enumerate(verdict_data):
        if i == 0:
            wrapped.append([Paragraph(c, cell_head) for c in row])
        else:
            wrapped.append([
                Paragraph(row[0], cell_verd),
                Paragraph(row[1], cell_style),
                Paragraph(row[2], cell_verd),
                Paragraph(row[3], cell_style),
            ])
    cw = [2.4*cm, 5.8*cm, 2.6*cm, 5.8*cm]
    t = Table(wrapped, colWidths=cw, repeatRows=1)
    t.setStyle(TableStyle([
        ('BACKGROUND', (0,0), (-1,0), HEADER_FILL),
        ('TEXTCOLOR',  (0,0), (-1,0), colors.white),
        ('VALIGN', (0,0), (-1,-1), 'MIDDLE'),
        ('ALIGN', (0,0), (-1,0), 'CENTER'),
        ('ALIGN', (2,1), (2,-1), 'CENTER'),
        ('GRID', (0,0), (-1,-1), 0.5, BORDER),
        ('ROWBACKGROUNDS', (0,1), (-1,-1), [colors.white, TABLE_STRIPE]),
        ('TOPPADDING', (0,0), (-1,-1), 5),
        ('BOTTOMPADDING', (0,0), (-1,-1), 5),
        ('LEFTPADDING', (0,0), (-1,-1), 4),
        ('RIGHTPADDING', (0,0), (-1,-1), 4),
    ]))
    story.append(t)
    story.append(S(8))

    story.append(Paragraph("5.2  Recommended next iterations", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "The experiments above are deliberately small and CPU-feasible. The "
        "results are clean enough to support three concrete follow-ups:", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>1. Test the Leech lattice in its native 24-dimensional real form, "
        "not the F<sub>2</sub> quotient.</b> The current Experiment A+C shows "
        "the F<sub>2</sub> binary quotient adds nothing beyond a random linear "
        "projection. But the Leech lattice's geometric richness lives in its "
        "196,560 minimal vectors as points in R<sup>24</sup>, with a Euclidean "
        "metric and an exceptional automorphism group Co<sub>0</sub>. A real "
        "follow-up would: encode each binary grid as a real 24-vector (via "
        "&#177;1 lifting), find the nearest Leech lattice point, and search "
        "for a shortest-minimal-vector path between the encoded demo states. "
        "Apply that path as a sequence of minimal-vector additions to the "
        "encoded query, then snap back to a binary grid. This is a much "
        "more expensive experiment but it is the only way to test Note 5's "
        "geodesic-of-thought hypothesis honestly.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>2. Scale Experiment B to a larger architecture and to RE-ARC "
        "samples.</b> The current trace-supervision result is cleanest on the "
        "toggle task because the task encoding z is constant, which makes "
        "the gradient signal unambiguous. To verify whether the benefit "
        "generalises, the next step is to (a) increase the state dimension "
        "(64 -> 256), (b) increase the recurrence depth (T=2 -> T=8), and "
        "(c) move from synthetic 4x6 binary tasks to RE-ARC task families "
        "where the intermediate milestones are produced by instrumented "
        "Python generators (per Note 3's recommendation). This will require "
        "GPU and a longer run, but it is the experiment that the notes "
        "explicitly call for.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>3. Add the Note 5 disambiguation test as a controlled "
        "intervention.</b> Note 5 explicitly recommends: <i>'change an "
        "object's size so a different object becomes the smallest. Does the "
        "selection switch? Move the container. Does the destination change?'</i> "
        "This is a stronger test than transfer accuracy -- it tests whether the "
        "model has learned the <i>operator</i> rather than memorised the demo. "
        "The current experiments measure transfer to held-out queries from "
        "the same generator; the next iteration should measure transfer to "
        "intervention-modified queries, where the rule is preserved but the "
        "object configuration is perturbed.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("5.3  What did NOT work", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Two design choices that did not work and are worth flagging for the "
        "next iteration: (1) The original Experiment B used a 4-step sigmoid-"
        "recurrence with vanilla SGD; gradient saturation through four "
        "sigmoid applications killed the gradient and the model failed to "
        "learn even toggle. Switching to a 2-step tanh recurrence with "
        "manual backprop-through-time fixed this. (2) The initial task "
        "encoding z = y<sub>1</sub> - x<sub>1</sub> (signed difference) was "
        "uninformative for toggle because the resulting z is a random &#177;1 "
        "pattern; switching to z = y<sub>1</sub> &#8853; x<sub>1</sub> (the "
        "F<sub>2</sub> displacement) made the toggle task encoding constant "
        "and learnable. This is itself a small confirmation of Note 5's "
        "warning that <i>a state difference is not generally the rule</i>: "
        "for toggle, the F<sub>2</sub> difference IS invariant and is the "
        "right encoding; for translate, neither the signed difference nor "
        "the XOR difference is invariant, and the model has to learn the "
        "operator from the structure of the demo, not from a per-cell "
        "displacement.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("5.4  Limitations", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "All experiments were CPU-only, on 4x6 binary grids with at most 500 "
        "training examples per task. The substrate calls (especially the "
        "Leech minimal-vector enumeration, the Construction-B/C ladder, and "
        "the Steiner S(5,8,24) verification) are exact and exhaustive, so "
        "the Note 4 audit is fully powered. The Note 5 conclusion (Golay-"
        "coset = random-linear in 249/250 trials) is a strong-statistical-"
        "significance result on the chosen task families; it would be worth "
        "re-running on a larger and more diverse task set to confirm. The "
        "Experiment B result (TRACE > FINAL_ONLY on toggle) is a 3-seed "
        "result on a small architecture; while the effect size is large "
        "(+0.157 EM, std &#8776; 0.05) and the mechanism is consistent with "
        "Note 2, scaling to a larger architecture and to RE-ARC samples is "
        "needed before treating this as a definitive confirmation.", body))

    return story


def main():
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(OUT_PDF), exist_ok=True)
    doc = SimpleDocTemplate(OUT_PDF, pagesize=A4,
                              leftMargin=2*cm, rightMargin=2*cm,
                              topMargin=2*cm, bottomMargin=2*cm,
                              title="GLM Computational Experiment Report",
                              author="Z.ai",
                              subject="Computational experiment testing Note 5 and Notes 1-2 of the GLM ARC notes",
                              creator="Z.ai")
    story = build_story()
    doc.build(story, onFirstPage=_draw_cover, onLaterPages=_draw_page)
    print(f"Wrote: {OUT_PDF}")
    print(f"Size: {os.path.getsize(OUT_PDF)/1024:.1f} KB")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

bin_tasks.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Synthetic binary task generators for the GLM experiments.

Each task family exposes:
    generate(seed, H, W, ...) -> (input_grid, output_grid, intermediate_states)

where intermediate_states is a list of (label, grid) tuples in execution order,
matching the "verified transformation traces" requirement of TraceViT / Note 2.

All grids are numpy 2D arrays of {0,1} (uint8). Pure substrate, no ARC dependency.

Task families:
    toggle        - y = NOT x.        Δ_x = all-ones (constant) — DISPLACEMENT TRANSFERS.
    translate     - move a 1-block by (dx,dy). Δ depends on position — DISPLACEMENT FAILS.
    reflect_v     - y = mirror across vertical axis. Δ depends on contents — DISPLACEMENT FAILS.
    flood_fill    - fill enclosed region. Δ depends on geometry — DISPLACEMENT FAILS.
    bfs_shortest  - mark shortest path between two marked bits — DISPLACEMENT FAILS.

Usage:
    from scripts.bin_tasks import TASKS, sample_task
    task = TASKS["translate"]
    x, y, trace = task.generate(seed=0, H=8, W=8, dx=2, dy=1)
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import sys, os
from typing import List, Tuple, Dict, Callable, Any
import numpy as np

Grid = np.ndarray   # shape (H, W), dtype uint8, values in {0,1}

def _new_grid(H: int, W: int) -> Grid:
    return np.zeros((H, W), dtype=np.uint8)

def _random_block(seed: int, H: int, W: int, block_h: int = 2, block_w: int = 2):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # leave a 1-cell margin so translations can move within bounds
    top = rng.integers(1, max(2, H - block_h))
    left = rng.integers(1, max(2, W - block_w))
    return int(top), int(left)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Task: TOGGLE  -- y = NOT x.  Δ_x is the all-ones grid (constant, transfers).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def gen_toggle(seed: int, H: int = 8, W: int = 8, **kw) -> Tuple[Grid, Grid, List[Tuple[str, Grid]]]:
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x = rng.integers(0, 2, size=(H, W)).astype(np.uint8)
    y = (1 - x).astype(np.uint8)
    trace = [("init", x.copy()), ("bitwise_not", y.copy())]
    return x, y, trace

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Task: TRANSLATE -- place a single 2x2 block, then move it by (dx, dy).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def gen_translate(seed: int, H: int = 8, W: int = 8,
                  block_h: int = 2, block_w: int = 2,
                  dx: int = 0, dy: int = 0, **kw) -> Tuple[Grid, Grid, List[Tuple[str, Grid]]]:
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x = _new_grid(H, W)
    # choose a position with room to translate by (dy, dx)
    if dy >= 0:
        top = rng.integers(1, max(2, H - block_h - dy))
    else:
        top = rng.integers(1 - dy, max(2 - dy, H - block_h))
    if dx >= 0:
        left = rng.integers(1, max(2, W - block_w - dx))
    else:
        left = rng.integers(1 - dx, max(2 - dx, W - block_w))
    x[top:top+block_h, left:left+block_w] = 1
    # also a "floor" indicator row at the bottom (a 1xW line of 1s)
    if H >= 4:
        x[H-1, :] = 1
    y = x.copy()
    # erase the block at the old position
    y[top:top+block_h, left:left+block_w] = 0
    # paint it at the new position
    new_top, new_left = top + dy, left + dx
    y[new_top:new_top+block_h, new_left:new_left+block_w] = 1
    # restore floor
    if H >= 4:
        y[H-1, :] = 1
    # trace: init -> mask_block -> erase_old -> paint_new
    mask = _new_grid(H, W)
    mask[top:top+block_h, left:left+block_w] = 1
    erased = x.copy()
    erased[top:top+block_h, left:left+block_w] = 0
    if H >= 4:
        erased[H-1, :] = 1
    painted = erased.copy()
    painted[new_top:new_top+block_h, new_left:new_left+block_w] = 1
    if H >= 4:
        painted[H-1, :] = 1
    trace = [("init", x.copy()),
             ("mask_block", mask),
             ("erase_old", erased),
             ("paint_new", painted)]
    return x, y, trace

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Task: REFLECT_V -- mirror across the vertical centre axis.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def gen_reflect_v(seed: int, H: int = 8, W: int = 8, **kw) -> Tuple[Grid, Grid, List[Tuple[str, Grid]]]:
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # Build a non-symmetric grid so the reflection is non-trivial.
    # Place a few random small blocks at distinct positions.
    x = _new_grid(H, W)
    n_blocks = int(rng.integers(2, 5))
    positions = []
    for _ in range(n_blocks):
        bh = int(rng.integers(1, 3))
        bw = int(rng.integers(1, 3))
        t = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, H - bh + 1)))
        l = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, W - bw + 1)))
        x[t:t+bh, l:l+bw] = 1
        positions.append((t, l, bh, bw))
    y = x[:, ::-1].copy()
    trace = [("init", x.copy()), ("flip_horizontal", y.copy())]
    return x, y, trace

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Task: FLOOD_FILL -- fill the interior of a closed boundary.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def gen_flood_fill(seed: int, H: int = 8, W: int = 8, **kw) -> Tuple[Grid, Grid, List[Tuple[str, Grid]]]:
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x = _new_grid(H, W)
    # Draw a closed rectangular boundary of 1s with empty interior.
    # Vary size and position so the rule is non-trivial.
    h = int(rng.integers(max(3, H // 2), H))
    w = int(rng.integers(max(3, W // 2), W))
    top = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, H - h)))
    left = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, W - w)))
    # boundary
    x[top, left:left+w] = 1
    x[top+h-1, left:left+w] = 1
    x[top:top+h, left] = 1
    x[top:top+h, left+w-1] = 1
    # interior is empty (0) -- this is the "unfilled" state
    # Output: fill the interior
    y = x.copy()
    if h > 2 and w > 2:
        y[top+1:top+h-1, left+1:left+w-1] = 1
    trace = [("init", x.copy()), ("fill_interior", y.copy())]
    return x, y, trace

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Task: BFS_SHORTEST -- given a start bit and an end bit on a 0-grid, mark the
# shortest Manhattan path between them.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def gen_bfs_shortest(seed: int, H: int = 8, W: int = 8, **kw) -> Tuple[Grid, Grid, List[Tuple[str, Grid]]]:
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x = _new_grid(H, W)
    # Place two distinct marked cells
    cells = set()
    while len(cells) < 2:
        r = int(rng.integers(0, H))
        c = int(rng.integers(0, W))
        cells.add((r, c))
    (r1, c1), (r2, c2) = sorted(cells)
    x[r1, c1] = 1
    x[r2, c2] = 1
    # Build y: mark the L-shaped shortest path
    y = x.copy()
    # Move vertically first, then horizontally
    step_r = 1 if r2 > r1 else -1
    step_c = 1 if c2 > c1 else -1
    trace_grids = [("init", x.copy())]
    # Vertical segment
    cur_r = r1
    intermediate = x.copy()
    while cur_r != r2:
        cur_r += step_r
        intermediate[cur_r, c1] = 1
        trace_grids.append(("step_vertical", intermediate.copy()))
    # Horizontal segment
    cur_c = c1
    while cur_c != c2:
        cur_c += step_c
        intermediate[cur_r, cur_c] = 1
        trace_grids.append(("step_horizontal", intermediate.copy()))
    y = intermediate.copy()
    trace_grids.append(("final", y.copy()))
    return x, y, trace_grids

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Registry
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
class TaskFamily:
    def __init__(self, name: str, gen: Callable, transfer_safe: bool = False,
                 description: str = ""):
        self.name = name
        self.gen = gen
        self.transfer_safe = transfer_safe  # True if Δ_x transfers (only toggle)
        self.description = description
    def generate(self, seed: int, H: int = 8, W: int = 8, **kw):
        return self.gen(seed=seed, H=H, W=W, **kw)

TASKS: Dict[str, TaskFamily] = {
    "toggle":      TaskFamily("toggle",      gen_toggle,      transfer_safe=True,
                               description="y = NOT x; Δ_x is constant all-ones (displacement transfers)."),
    "translate":   TaskFamily("translate",   gen_translate,   transfer_safe=False,
                               description="Move a 2x2 block by (dx,dy); Δ depends on block position."),
    "reflect_v":   TaskFamily("reflect_v",   gen_reflect_v,   transfer_safe=False,
                               description="Mirror across vertical axis; Δ depends on contents."),
    "flood_fill":  TaskFamily("flood_fill",  gen_flood_fill,  transfer_safe=False,
                               description="Fill interior of closed boundary; Δ depends on geometry."),
    "bfs_shortest":TaskFamily("bfs_shortest",gen_bfs_shortest,transfer_safe=False,
                               description="Mark shortest Manhattan path between two bits."),
}


def sample_task(name: str, seed: int, H: int = 8, W: int = 8, **kw):
    return TASKS[name].generate(seed=seed, H=H, W=W, **kw)


def make_demo_set(name: str, n: int, H: int = 8, W: int = 8,
                  seed_offset: int = 0, **kw) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """Produce n demonstrations of one task family, each as {x, y, trace}."""
    out: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
    for i in range(n):
        x, y, trace = sample_task(name, seed=seed_offset + i, H=H, W=W, **kw)
        out.append({"x": x, "y": y, "trace": trace,
                    "H": H, "W": W, "task": name, "seed": seed_offset + i})
    return out


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # quick smoke test
    for name, fam in TASKS.items():
        x, y, trace = fam.generate(seed=0, H=8, W=8)
        print(f"{name:14s} H={x.shape[0]} W={x.shape[1]} trace_len={len(trace)} "
              f"transfer_safe={fam.transfer_safe}")
    print("OK")


====

iter2_01_cost_atlas.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Phase 1 (iteration 2) — Golay-syndrome cost atlas.

The user's clarification (a): "The Golay code isn't just for error correction,
when we don't want to change data we use it as a measuring device to see the
cost and inner working of any given data transaction."

So for every transition x -> y, we measure:
  * F_2 cost: the syndrome weight of (x XOR y) — the coset weight of the
    displacement, in {0,1,2,3,4} for the Golay code (4 = deep hole).
  * TAX cost: if the displacement's nearest codeword has Hamming weight w,
    TAX = w * (Y + 1/8) where Y = 1/(pi + 2/pi) ≈ 0.2647.
    The minimal codeword TAX is 8Y + 1 = 3.1174 (the 759 octads).
  * Symmetry tax of x and y individually (the codeword weight of x, y
    if they are themselves codewords; else 0).

Outputs:
  /home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase1_atlas.{json,txt}
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import sys, os, json
from fractions import Fraction
from typing import Dict, List, Any, Tuple
import numpy as np

GLM_REPO = "/home/z/my-project/glm_repo"
sys.path.insert(0, GLM_REPO)
sys.path.insert(0, "/home/z/my-project/scripts")

import warnings
warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=SyntaxWarning)

from glm_universal.substrate import leech2, mog, golay_decode, linalg
from bin_tasks import TASKS

OUT_JSON = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase1_atlas.json"
OUT_TXT  = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase1_atlas.txt"

# The Y constant from the GLM academic paper (§9.1):
#   Y = 1/(pi + 2/pi)
# We use exact rational approximation via Fraction. The paper uses
# pi ≈ 355/113 (a continued-fraction convergent); we keep Y as the
# same approximation the substrate would: a Fraction.
Y_CONST = Fraction(1) / (Fraction(355, 113) + Fraction(2) / Fraction(355, 113))
# TAX per weight unit
TAX_PER_W = Y_CONST + Fraction(1, 8)


def grid_to_bits_24(grid: np.ndarray) -> int:
    H, W = grid.shape
    assert H * W == 24, f"grid must pack to 24 bits, got {H}x{W}={H*W}"
    bits = 0
    for v in grid.flatten():
        bits = (bits << 1) | int(v & 1)
    return bits


def transition_cost(x_bits: int, y_bits: int) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Measure the F_2 + TAX cost of the transition x -> y."""
    delta = x_bits ^ y_bits
    # Syndrome of the delta
    dec = golay_decode.decode_complete(delta)
    syn = dec.syndrome
    coset_w = dec.weight          # 0..4 (coset leader weight)
    # The nearest codeword to delta has weight = popcount(corrected) if
    # unambiguous, else None. For TAX we want the WEIGHT of the codeword
    # (which is a measure of the "energy" of the displacement as a codeword).
    # The substrate's decoder gives `corrected` as the codeword; in ambiguous
    # cases (coset_w=4) it returns None and we pick the lowest-weight candidate.
    if dec.corrected is not None:
        cw = dec.corrected
        cw_w = linalg.popcount(cw)
    else:
        # pick the candidate with the smallest weight (deterministic)
        cands = dec.candidates if hasattr(dec, "candidates") else None
        if cands:
            cw = min(cands, key=lambda c: (linalg.popcount(c), c))
            cw_w = linalg.popcount(cw)
        else:
            cw, cw_w = None, None
    # Also: the displacement itself has a Hamming weight (number of changed cells)
    delta_w = linalg.popcount(delta)
    # TAX
    tax = (cw_w * TAX_PER_W) if cw_w is not None else None
    return {
        "delta_weight": delta_w,
        "coset_weight": coset_w,
        "syndrome": syn,
        "codeword_weight": cw_w,
        "tax": float(tax) if tax is not None else None,
        "tax_exact_str": f"{tax.numerator}/{tax.denominator}" if tax is not None else None,
        "ambiguous": (coset_w == 4),
        "is_codeword_delta": (coset_w == 0),
    }


def per_state_cost(bits: int) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Cost of the state itself: its distance to the nearest codeword (F_2 cost
    of "holding" this state)."""
    dec = golay_decode.decode_complete(bits)
    return {
        "coset_weight": dec.weight,
        "syndrome": dec.syndrome,
        "ambiguous": (dec.weight == 4),
    }


def measure_task_family(name: str, n_samples: int = 100, H: int = 4, W: int = 6) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Measure transition costs across n_samples demonstrations of one task family."""
    fam = TASKS[name]
    rows = []
    for i in range(n_samples):
        x, y, trace = fam.generate(seed=i + 1, H=H, W=W, dx=1, dy=1)
        x_b = grid_to_bits_24(x)
        y_b = grid_to_bits_24(y)
        # Full transition cost
        tc = transition_cost(x_b, y_b)
        # State costs
        x_state = per_state_cost(x_b)
        y_state = per_state_cost(y_b)
        # Intermediate steps: cost of each transition in the trace
        trace_costs = []
        prev = x_b
        for label, grid in trace:
            gb = grid_to_bits_24(grid) if grid.size == 24 else None
            if gb is None:
                continue
            tc_step = transition_cost(prev, gb)
            trace_costs.append({"step": label, "from": prev, "to": gb, **tc_step})
            prev = gb
        rows.append({
            "seed": i + 1, "x_bits": x_b, "y_bits": y_b,
            "transition_cost": tc,
            "x_state_cost": x_state,
            "y_state_cost": y_state,
            "trace_costs": trace_costs,
        })
    # Aggregate
    deltas = [r["transition_cost"]["delta_weight"] for r in rows]
    cosets = [r["transition_cost"]["coset_weight"] for r in rows]
    taxes  = [r["transition_cost"]["tax"] for r in rows if r["transition_cost"]["tax"] is not None]
    ambig  = sum(1 for r in rows if r["transition_cost"]["ambiguous"])
    cw_weights = [r["transition_cost"]["codeword_weight"] for r in rows
                   if r["transition_cost"]["codeword_weight"] is not None]
    # syndrome histogram
    syndromes = [r["transition_cost"]["syndrome"] for r in rows]
    n_unique_syndromes = len(set(syndromes))
    return {
        "task": name,
        "n_samples": n_samples,
        "delta_weight_mean": float(np.mean(deltas)),
        "delta_weight_std":  float(np.std(deltas)),
        "coset_weight_mean": float(np.mean(cosets)),
        "coset_weight_std":  float(np.std(cosets)),
        "coset_weight_histogram": {int(k): int(v) for k, v in np.histogram(cosets, bins=range(6))[0]
                                    .tolist() and
                                    zip(range(5), np.histogram(cosets, bins=range(6))[0])},
        "tax_mean": float(np.mean(taxes)) if taxes else None,
        "tax_std":  float(np.std(taxes))  if taxes else None,
        "ambiguous_count": ambig,
        "ambiguous_rate": ambig / n_samples,
        "codeword_weight_histogram": {int(k): int(v) for k, v in
                                       zip(*np.unique(cw_weights, return_counts=True))
                                       if cw_weights},
        "unique_syndromes": n_unique_syndromes,
        "syndrome_concentration": float(max(np.bincount(syndromes))) / n_samples,
        "rows": rows,
    }


def render(atlas: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    lines = []
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("ITERATION 2 / PHASE 1 — Golay-syndrome cost atlas")
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("Golay as a measuring device: for each transition x -> y on a 4x6 grid (24 bits),")
    lines.append("measure the F_2 cost (coset weight, syndrome) and the TAX cost (codeword weight")
    lines.append(f"times Y + 1/8, where Y = 1/(pi + 2/pi) = {float(Y_CONST):.6f}).")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append(f"{'Task':<14}{'Δ wt':<14}{'coset wt':<16}{'TAX':<14}{'ambig%':<10}{'unique synd':<14}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    for name, agg in atlas.items():
        lines.append(
            f"{name:<14}"
            f"{agg['delta_weight_mean']:.2f}±{agg['delta_weight_std']:.2f}    "
            f"{agg['coset_weight_mean']:.2f}±{agg['coset_weight_std']:.2f}    "
            f"{agg['tax_mean']:.4f}     "
            f"{100*agg['ambiguous_rate']:.1f}%    "
            f"{agg['unique_syndromes']}"
        )
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("Interpretation:")
    lines.append("  * coset wt 0 = the displacement is itself a Golay codeword (an octad or 0).")
    lines.append("    This means the F_2 quotient 'sees' the transition as a no-op; the rule is")
    lines.append("    invisible at the binary substrate level (Note 5's failure mode).")
    lines.append("  * coset wt 4 = the displacement sits at a deep hole (sextet ambiguity).")
    lines.append("    The substrate cannot decide which octad to credit the change to. This is an")
    lines.append("    escalation trigger — the substrate must lift to a higher layer to disambiguate.")
    lines.append("  * TAX is the per-transition 'energy' on the substrate. The minimum non-zero TAX")
    lines.append("    is at octads (weight 8 codewords), TAX = 8Y + 1 = 3.1174. A transition with")
    lines.append("    TAX > 3.1174 costs more; a transition with coset_wt 0 AND codeword wt 0 is the")
    lines.append("    identity (no movement).")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("Per-task signatures:")
    for name, agg in atlas.items():
        lines.append(f"  {name}:")
        lines.append(f"    coset weight histogram: {agg['coset_weight_histogram']}")
        lines.append(f"    codeword weight histogram: {agg['codeword_weight_histogram']}")
        lines.append(f"    unique syndromes: {agg['unique_syndromes']} (concentration: {agg['syndrome_concentration']:.3f})")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def main():
    os.makedirs("/home/z/my-project/data", exist_ok=True)
    atlas = {}
    print("Phase 1: Golay-syndrome cost atlas (100 samples per task family)...")
    for name in ["toggle", "translate", "reflect_v", "flood_fill", "bfs_shortest"]:
        print(f"  measuring {name}...", end=" ", flush=True)
        atlas[name] = measure_task_family(name, n_samples=100)
        print(f"mean coset wt = {atlas[name]['coset_weight_mean']:.2f}, "
              f"unique syndromes = {atlas[name]['unique_syndromes']}")
    txt = render(atlas)
    with open(OUT_TXT, "w") as f:
        f.write(txt)
    with open(OUT_JSON, "w") as f:
        json.dump(atlas, f, indent=2, default=str)
    print()
    print(txt)
    print(f"\nWrote: {OUT_JSON}")
    print(f"Wrote: {OUT_TXT}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

iter2_02_wobble.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Phase 2 (iteration 2) — Delta-sigma wobble signatures.

The user's clarification (b): "looking simply at the difference between any
two data changes is not enough, we also need to look at the path it takes,
not just the start/end points or nodes - what is the exact shape of this
'decision'."

The substrate exposes `golay_delta_sigma(target, steps, rule)` which runs a
deterministic error-feedback loop against the 4,096 Golay codewords. For each
task family, we use the QUERY GRID (the input we want to transform) as the
24-coordinate target, run the wobble for N=200 steps, and record:

  * trajectory length
  * unique codewords visited
  * most_visited_share  (how concentrated the orbit is)
  * mean_distance       (mean L1 distance from the emitted codeword to target)
  * max_coordinate_deviation (does the running average converge?)
  * within_one_over_n   (is the 1/N bound respected?)
  * ambiguous_ticks     (how often the decoder hit a sextet tie)
  * max_accumulator     (the worst excursion)

Each task family should have a CHARACTERISTIC wobble signature. We also run
the wobble on known constants (√2, π, e, 1/3) as a reference baseline — the
GLM paper reports these have distinct "vibrational signatures".

Outputs:
  /home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase2_wobble.{json,txt}
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import sys, os, json
from fractions import Fraction
from typing import Dict, List, Any, Tuple
import numpy as np

GLM_REPO = "/home/z/my-project/glm_repo"
sys.path.insert(0, GLM_REPO)
sys.path.insert(0, "/home/z/my-project/scripts")

import warnings
warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=SyntaxWarning)

from glm_universal.reasoning import exact_real
from glm_universal.substrate import leech2, mog, golay_decode, linalg
from bin_tasks import TASKS

OUT_JSON = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase2_wobble.json"
OUT_TXT  = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase2_wobble.txt"


def grid_to_target_24(grid: np.ndarray) -> Tuple[Fraction, ...]:
    """Map a binary grid to a 24-coordinate target in [0,1]^24 (one coord per cell)."""
    H, W = grid.shape
    assert H * W == 24
    # Each cell becomes its own coordinate; the binary value 0/1 maps to 0/1 directly.
    # (We could also use ±1/2 to center the alphabet; the substrate's delta_sigma
    # accepts any rational in [0,1].)
    return tuple(Fraction(int(v)) for v in grid.flatten())


def run_wobble(name: str, target: Tuple[Fraction, ...], steps: int = 200,
                rule: str = "nearest") -> Dict[str, Any]:
    res = exact_real.golay_delta_sigma(target, steps, rule=rule)
    # Drop the heavy trajectory for storage; keep summary stats
    return {
        "task": name,
        "rule": rule,
        "steps": res["steps"],
        "unique_codewords": res["unique_codewords"],
        "most_visited_share": float(res["most_visited_share"]),
        "mean_distance": float(res["mean_distance"]),
        "max_coordinate_deviation": float(res["max_coordinate_deviation"]),
        "within_one_over_n": bool(res["within_one_over_n"]),
        "ambiguous_ticks": res["ambiguous_ticks"],
        "max_accumulator": float(res["max_accumulator"]),
        # also: entropy of the visit distribution
        # (we'll re-derive this from the trajectory, which we kept)
    }


def run_hull(target: Tuple[Fraction, ...], steps: int = 400) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Run hull_certificate to test whether the target lies inside the convex hull
    of the 4096 codewords. If 'separates' is True, the target is provably
    UNREACHABLE by ANY quantiser rule."""
    cert = exact_real.hull_certificate(target, steps)
    return {
        "value_at_target": float(cert["value_at_target"]),
        "max_over_codewords": float(cert["max_over_codewords"]),
        "separates": bool(cert["separates"]),
        "gap": float(cert["gap"]),
        "steps": cert["steps"],
    }


def known_constant_target(name: str) -> Tuple[Fraction, ...]:
    """Build a 24-coordinate target from a known constant.

    We replicate the paper's setup (§5.4): the fractional target is placed
    in ALL 24 coordinates, so the wobble's task is to track a single scalar
    constant using the 24-bit alphabet.
    """
    if name == "1/3":
        f = Fraction(1, 3)
    elif name == "sqrt2_minus_1":
        # use 414214/1000000 as the rational approximation
        f = Fraction(414214, 1000000)
    elif name == "pi_minus_3":
        f = Fraction(141593, 1000000)
    elif name == "e_minus_2":
        f = Fraction(718282, 1000000)
    elif name == "phi_minus_1":
        f = Fraction(618034, 1000000)
    elif name == "0":
        f = Fraction(0)
    else:
        raise ValueError(name)
    return tuple(f for _ in range(24))


def main():
    os.makedirs("/home/z/my-project/data", exist_ok=True)
    print("Phase 2: Delta-sigma wobble signatures")
    # (a) constants baseline
    constants = ["0", "1/3", "sqrt2_minus_1", "pi_minus_3", "e_minus_2", "phi_minus_1"]
    print("\n[Constants baseline]")
    const_results = {}
    for c in constants:
        target = known_constant_target(c)
        w = run_wobble(f"const:{c}", target, steps=400)
        h = run_hull(target, steps=200)
        const_results[c] = {"wobble": w, "hull": h}
        print(f"  {c:18s} unique_cw={w['unique_codewords']:4d}  "
              f"share={w['most_visited_share']:.3f}  "
              f"dev={w['max_coordinate_deviation']:.4f}  "
              f"ambig={w['ambiguous_ticks']:3d}  "
              f"hull_separates={h['separates']}")

    # (b) task families
    print("\n[Task families — query grid as target]")
    task_results = {}
    for name in ["toggle", "translate", "reflect_v", "flood_fill", "bfs_shortest"]:
        fam = TASKS[name]
        # Run on 20 different query grids and aggregate
        per_seed = []
        for s in range(20):
            x_q, y_q, _ = fam.generate(seed=s + 100, H=4, W=6, dx=1, dy=1)
            target = grid_to_target_24(x_q)
            w = run_wobble(f"{name}_q{s}", target, steps=400)
            h = run_hull(target, steps=200)
            per_seed.append({"seed": s, "wobble": w, "hull": h})
        # Aggregate
        uniq = [p["wobble"]["unique_codewords"] for p in per_seed]
        share = [p["wobble"]["most_visited_share"] for p in per_seed]
        dev = [p["wobble"]["max_coordinate_deviation"] for p in per_seed]
        ambig = [p["wobble"]["ambiguous_ticks"] for p in per_seed]
        acc = [p["wobble"]["max_accumulator"] for p in per_seed]
        sep_count = sum(1 for p in per_seed if p["hull"]["separates"])
        task_results[name] = {
            "per_seed_summary": {
                "unique_codewords_mean": float(np.mean(uniq)),
                "unique_codewords_std": float(np.std(uniq)),
                "most_visited_share_mean": float(np.mean(share)),
                "max_coordinate_deviation_mean": float(np.mean(dev)),
                "ambiguous_ticks_mean": float(np.mean(ambig)),
                "max_accumulator_mean": float(np.mean(acc)),
                "hull_separates_rate": sep_count / 20,
            },
            "per_seed": per_seed,
        }
        s = task_results[name]["per_seed_summary"]
        print(f"  {name:14s} unique={s['unique_codewords_mean']:6.1f}±{s['unique_codewords_std']:.1f}  "
              f"share={s['most_visited_share_mean']:.3f}  "
              f"dev={s['max_coordinate_deviation_mean']:.4f}  "
              f"ambig={s['ambiguous_ticks_mean']:.1f}  "
              f"acc_max={s['max_accumulator_mean']:.2f}  "
              f"hull_sep={100*s['hull_separates_rate']:.0f}%")

    # Render text
    lines = []
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("ITERATION 2 / PHASE 2 — Delta-sigma wobble signatures")
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("Golay delta-sigma modulator (the 'wobble'): for each 24-coordinate target,")
    lines.append("run the substrate's exact_real.golay_delta_sigma for 400 ticks. The")
    lines.append("trajectory visits codewords; the accumulator tracks target error.")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("[Constants baseline] (24 coordinates all = the fractional target)")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append(f"{'Constant':<20}{'unique cw':<12}{'most share':<14}{'max dev':<14}{'ambig':<10}{'hull sep':<12}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    for c, r in const_results.items():
        w = r["wobble"]; h = r["hull"]
        lines.append(f"{c:<20}{w['unique_codewords']:<12}{w['most_visited_share']:<14.3f}"
                     f"{w['max_coordinate_deviation']:<14.4f}{w['ambiguous_ticks']:<10}"
                     f"{str(h['separates']):<12}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("[Task families — query grid flattened to 24-bit target]")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append(f"{'Task':<14}{'unique cw':<14}{'most share':<14}{'max dev':<14}{'ambig':<10}{'hull sep%':<12}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    for name, r in task_results.items():
        s = r["per_seed_summary"]
        lines.append(f"{name:<14}"
                     f"{s['unique_codewords_mean']:6.1f}±{s['unique_codewords_std']:<3.1f}    "
                     f"{s['most_visited_share_mean']:<14.3f}"
                     f"{s['max_coordinate_deviation_mean']:<14.4f}"
                     f"{s['ambiguous_ticks_mean']:<10.1f}"
                     f"{100*s['hull_separates_rate']:<12.0f}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("Interpretation:")
    lines.append("  * 'unique cw' = number of distinct codewords the wobble visits.")
    lines.append("    High -> orbit is diverse; the target is far from any codeword.")
    lines.append("    Low  -> orbit concentrates on a few codewords (target IS close to a")
    lines.append("            codeword, or the substrate has a periodic attractor there).")
    lines.append("  * 'most share' = the fraction of ticks spent at the most-visited codeword.")
    lines.append("    1.0 = stuck at a single codeword (target IS a codeword).")
    lines.append("  * 'max dev' = the worst coordinate-wise deviation of the running average")
    lines.append("    from the target. If within 1/400 = 0.0025, the 1/N bound holds.")
    lines.append("  * 'hull sep' = True iff the target lies OUTSIDE the convex hull of the")
    lines.append("    4,096 codewords. Such targets are PROVABLY UNREACHABLE by any")
    lines.append("    quantiser rule — the substrate must escalate to a higher lattice.")
    txt = "\n".join(lines)
    with open(OUT_TXT, "w") as f:
        f.write(txt)
    with open(OUT_JSON, "w") as f:
        json.dump({"constants": const_results, "tasks": task_results},
                   f, indent=2, default=str)
    print()
    print(txt)
    print(f"\nWrote: {OUT_JSON}")
    print(f"Wrote: {OUT_TXT}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

iter2_03_leech_wobble.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Phase 3 (iteration 2) — Leech native 24-D wobble.

The user's clarification (d): "The Leech in its native 24-D is the natural
progression, according to GLM findings there is a layered perspective to
almost all things where failure indicated the required mechanism for
progression up the ladder."

Phase 2 found that ALL 5 task-family query grids lie OUTSIDE the convex hull
of the 4096 Golay codewords — the Golay delta-sigma modulator provably cannot
reach them, and the accumulator grows linearly at the predicted rate.

This phase tests whether the Leech lattice's minimal vectors — 196,560 of
them, in R^24, NOT in F_2^24 — can reach those same targets.

We use the 'matched' rule (the paper's Rule 2, §8.4): at each tick, emit the
minimal vector v that maximises <v, accumulator + target>. Update the
accumulator by subtracting v. This is the support-function quantiser.

To keep CPU time tractable, we use the **1,104 sparse-shell minimal vectors
of shape (±4², 0²²)** as the alphabet. These are:
  * 276 unordered pairs of coordinates {i, j}, 24 choose 2
  * for each pair, 4 sign choices (+/+), (+/-), (-/+), (-/-)
  * = 276 * 4 = 1,104 vectors
Each has squared norm 32 (= 4² + 4²), the second-simplest Leech shell.

The 1104 shell is a strict subset of the full 196,560, but it is enough to
test whether a real-valued Leech alphabet can reach the binary targets the
Golay alphabet provably cannot.

Outputs:
  /home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase3_leech.{json,txt}
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import sys, os, json, time
from fractions import Fraction
from typing import Dict, List, Any, Tuple
import numpy as np

GLM_REPO = "/home/z/my-project/glm_repo"
sys.path.insert(0, GLM_REPO)
sys.path.insert(0, "/home/z/my-project/scripts")

import warnings
warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=SyntaxWarning)

from glm_universal.substrate import leech2
from bin_tasks import TASKS

OUT_JSON = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase3_leech.json"
OUT_TXT  = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase3_leech.txt"


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 1. Build the Leech alphabet (full 196,560 minimal vectors)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def build_full_leech_shell() -> np.ndarray:
    """All 196,560 Leech minimal vectors as a numpy array (196560 x 24).

    These are the three shapes:
      * 1,104 of (±4², 0²²)
      * 97,152 of (±2⁸ on an octad)
      * 98,304 of (∓3, ±1²³)
    Each has squared norm 32 in the x*sqrt(8) integer model.
    """
    vecs = list(leech2.minimal_vectors())
    arr = np.array(vecs, dtype=np.int64)
    assert arr.shape == (196560, 24), f"got {arr.shape}"
    return arr


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 2. Leech-shell delta-sigma modulator (matched rule)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def leech_shell_delta_sigma(target: np.ndarray, alphabet: np.ndarray,
                              steps: int = 200) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Run the matched-rule delta-sigma against the Leech shell alphabet.

    At each tick:
      driven = accumulator + target
      emit v* = argmax_v <v, driven>  (over the alphabet)
      accumulator -= v*
    The running mean of emitted vectors converges to target iff target is in
    the convex hull of the alphabet (support function bound).

    Returns:
      trajectory of (v_index, distance_to_target),
      final accumulator,
      average,
      max coordinate deviation,
      max accumulator,
      unique vectors visited,
      most-visited share,
    """
    acc = np.zeros(24, dtype=np.float64)
    totals = np.zeros(24, dtype=np.float64)
    visits = np.zeros(len(alphabet), dtype=np.int64)
    trajectory = []
    excursion = 0.0
    # Precompute alphabet norms for support-function bound
    # We don't normalise; just take argmax of <v, driven>
    for _ in range(steps):
        driven = acc + target
        # <v, driven> for all v: matrix-vector product
        scores = alphabet @ driven
        idx = int(np.argmax(scores))
        v = alphabet[idx]
        acc = acc + target - v
        excursion = max(excursion, float(np.abs(acc).max()))
        totals = totals + v.astype(np.float64)
        visits[idx] += 1
        # distance: L2 norm of (target - v) (per-tick emission error)
        dist = float(np.linalg.norm(target - v))
        trajectory.append((idx, dist))
    average = totals / steps
    deviation = float(np.max(np.abs(average - target)))
    return {
        "steps": steps,
        "unique_vectors": int(np.sum(visits > 0)),
        "most_visited_share": float(visits.max() / steps),
        "max_coordinate_deviation": deviation,
        "max_accumulator": excursion,
        # 1/N bound: deviation should be ≤ covering_radius / N
        "ambiguous_ticks": 0,  # the matched rule is never ambiguous (always picks argmax)
        "mean_distance": float(np.mean([d for _, d in trajectory])),
        "visits_histogram": {
            int(k): int(v) for k, v in zip(*np.unique(visits[visits > 0], return_counts=True))
        } if visits.any() else {},
    }


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 3. Run on tasks and constants
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def grid_to_target_real_24(grid: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Map a binary 4x6 grid to a 24-D real target.

    Each cell becomes a real coordinate. We map bit 0 -> -4 and bit 1 -> +4
    to match the alphabet's per-coordinate range {-4, -3, ..., +4}.
    """
    H, W = grid.shape
    assert H * W == 24
    return np.array([-4.0 if v == 0 else 4.0 for v in grid.flatten()],
                     dtype=np.float64)


def const_to_target_real_24(frac: Fraction) -> np.ndarray:
    """Build a 24-D real target from a constant: place the constant (as float)
    in all 24 coordinates, scaled to match the alphabet's range.

    The Leech minimal vectors have norms in {sqrt(32)}; their entries are in
    {-4, -3, -2, -1, 0, +1, +2, +3, +4}. We map the constant's fractional
    value to the same range: target = (2f - 1) * 4 = 8f - 4.
    """
    f = float(frac)
    val = 8 * f - 4
    return np.full(24, val, dtype=np.float64)


def main():
    os.makedirs("/home/z/my-project/data", exist_ok=True)
    print("Phase 3: Leech native 24-D wobble (full 196,560 minimal vectors)")
    print("Building full Leech shell alphabet...", end=" ", flush=True)
    alphabet = build_full_leech_shell()
    print(f"shape={alphabet.shape}")

    # Constants baseline (same as Phase 2, scaled to [-4, 4] range)
    constants = {
        "0":               Fraction(0),
        "1/3":             Fraction(1, 3),
        "sqrt2_minus_1":   Fraction(414214, 1000000),
        "pi_minus_3":      Fraction(141593, 1000000),
        "e_minus_2":       Fraction(718282, 1000000),
        "phi_minus_1":     Fraction(618034, 1000000),
    }
    print("\n[Constants baseline — 24-D real targets in [-4, +4]]")
    const_res = {}
    for name, frac in constants.items():
        target = const_to_target_real_24(frac)
        t0 = time.time()
        r = leech_shell_delta_sigma(target, alphabet, steps=200)
        t1 = time.time()
        const_res[name] = {"target_value": float(frac), **r, "time_s": t1 - t0}
        print(f"  {name:18s} unique_v={r['unique_vectors']:4d}  "
              f"share={r['most_visited_share']:.3f}  "
              f"dev={r['max_coordinate_deviation']:.4f}  "
              f"acc_max={r['max_accumulator']:.2f}  "
              f"mean_dist={r['mean_distance']:.3f}  "
              f"({t1-t0:.2f}s)")

    # Task families — query grid as target
    print("\n[Task families — query grid as 24-D real target, bipolar encoding]")
    task_res = {}
    for task_name in ["toggle", "translate", "reflect_v", "flood_fill", "bfs_shortest"]:
        fam = TASKS[task_name]
        per_seed = []
        t0_total = time.time()
        for s in range(20):
            x_q, y_q, _ = fam.generate(seed=s + 100, H=4, W=6, dx=1, dy=1)
            target = grid_to_target_real_24(x_q)
            r = leech_shell_delta_sigma(target, alphabet, steps=200)
            per_seed.append({"seed": s, **r})
        t1_total = time.time()
        # aggregate
        uv = [p["unique_vectors"] for p in per_seed]
        sh = [p["most_visited_share"] for p in per_seed]
        dv = [p["max_coordinate_deviation"] for p in per_seed]
        am = [p["max_accumulator"] for p in per_seed]
        md = [p["mean_distance"] for p in per_seed]
        task_res[task_name] = {
            "per_seed_summary": {
                "unique_vectors_mean": float(np.mean(uv)),
                "unique_vectors_std": float(np.std(uv)),
                "most_visited_share_mean": float(np.mean(sh)),
                "max_coordinate_deviation_mean": float(np.mean(dv)),
                "max_accumulator_mean": float(np.mean(am)),
                "mean_distance_mean": float(np.mean(md)),
            },
            "per_seed": per_seed,
            "total_time_s": t1_total - t0_total,
        }
        s = task_res[task_name]["per_seed_summary"]
        print(f"  {task_name:14s} unique_v={s['unique_vectors_mean']:6.1f}±{s['unique_vectors_std']:.1f}  "
              f"share={s['most_visited_share_mean']:.3f}  "
              f"dev={s['max_coordinate_deviation_mean']:.4f}  "
              f"acc_max={s['max_accumulator_mean']:.2f}  "
              f"mean_d={s['mean_distance_mean']:.3f}  "
              f"({t1_total - t0_total:.1f}s)")

    # Hull check (Leech 1104 shell): does the accumulator stay bounded?
    # If accumulator grows linearly, target is outside the hull.
    # We measure this by max_accumulator: if it's ~steps, linear growth.
    # We also compute the "stays bounded" predicate (acc_max < 10 means bounded).

    # Render
    lines = []
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("ITERATION 2 / PHASE 3 — Leech native 24-D wobble")
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("Matched-rule delta-sigma against the FULL Leech minimal-vector shell")
    lines.append("(196,560 vectors of shapes (±4², 0²²), (±2⁸ on octad), (∓3, ±1²³),")
    lines.append("each with norm² 32). This is the paper's Rule 2 (§8.4) against the whole shell.")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("For each target, the running mean of emitted vectors should converge to the")
    lines.append("target iff the target is in the convex hull of the alphabet.")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("[Constants baseline — 24-D targets in [-4, +4]]")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append(f"{'Constant':<20}{'unique v':<12}{'most share':<14}{'max dev':<14}{'acc_max':<14}{'mean_d':<12}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    for name, r in const_res.items():
        lines.append(f"{name:<20}{r['unique_vectors']:<12}{r['most_visited_share']:<14.3f}"
                     f"{r['max_coordinate_deviation']:<14.4f}{r['max_accumulator']:<14.2f}"
                     f"{r['mean_distance']:<12.3f}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("[Task families — query grid as 24-D target, bipolar ±1 encoding]")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append(f"{'Task':<14}{'unique v':<16}{'most share':<14}{'max dev':<14}{'acc_max':<14}{'mean_d':<12}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    for name, r in task_res.items():
        s = r["per_seed_summary"]
        lines.append(f"{name:<14}{s['unique_vectors_mean']:6.1f}±{s['unique_vectors_std']:<3.1f}    "
                     f"{s['most_visited_share_mean']:<14.3f}"
                     f"{s['max_coordinate_deviation_mean']:<14.4f}"
                     f"{s['max_accumulator_mean']:<14.2f}"
                     f"{s['mean_distance_mean']:<12.3f}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("Interpretation:")
    lines.append("  * If acc_max stays bounded (< ~10) and max_dev shrinks with N, the target")
    lines.append("    IS reachable by the Leech shell alphabet -- the 1/N bound holds.")
    lines.append("  * If acc_max grows linearly with steps (≈ 200 after 200 steps), the target")
    lines.append("    is OUTSIDE the Leech 1104-shell hull. Escalation to the FULL Leech")
    lines.append("    196,560-shell or to the 32-D Barnes-Wall lattice is required.")
    lines.append("  * Compare to Phase 2: the Golay alphabet could not reach ANY task target")
    lines.append("    (all 100% hull-separates). If the Leech 1104-shell CAN reach them, this")
    lines.append("    is a concrete demonstration of the layered-perspective progression.")
    txt = "\n".join(lines)
    with open(OUT_TXT, "w") as f:
        f.write(txt)
    with open(OUT_JSON, "w") as f:
        json.dump({"constants": const_res, "tasks": task_res}, f, indent=2, default=str)
    print()
    print(txt)
    print(f"\nWrote: {OUT_JSON}")
    print(f"Wrote: {OUT_TXT}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
