iter2_04_per_step.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Phase 4 (iteration 2) — Step-by-step cost in multi-step transformation traces.

The user's clarification (c): "intermediate steps are part of (b) here - we
need to take every operation step-by-step and explicitly measure, map and
investigate each step - this is the information the GLM is to learn from."

We already collected per-step trace costs in Phase 1. This phase analyses them:
for each multi-step task (translate: 4 steps; bfs_shortest: many steps), we
extract the per-step syndrome, codeword weight, TAX, and coset weight, and
visualise where the cost spikes — the "decision points" the GLM must learn.

Outputs:
  /home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase4_per_step.{json,txt}
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import sys, os, json
from fractions import Fraction
from typing import Dict, List, Any, Tuple
import numpy as np

GLM_REPO = "/home/z/my-project/glm_repo"
sys.path.insert(0, GLM_REPO)
sys.path.insert(0, "/home/z/my-project/scripts")

import warnings
warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=SyntaxWarning)

from glm_universal.substrate import leech2, mog, golay_decode, linalg
from bin_tasks import TASKS

OUT_JSON = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase4_per_step.json"
OUT_TXT  = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter2_phase4_per_step.txt"

# Y from §9.1 of the academic paper
Y_CONST = Fraction(1) / (Fraction(355, 113) + Fraction(2) / Fraction(355, 113))
TAX_PER_W = Y_CONST + Fraction(1, 8)


def grid_to_bits_24(grid: np.ndarray) -> int:
    H, W = grid.shape
    assert H * W == 24
    bits = 0
    for v in grid.flatten():
        bits = (bits << 1) | int(v & 1)
    return bits


def step_cost(x_bits: int, y_bits: int) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    delta = x_bits ^ y_bits
    dec = golay_decode.decode_complete(delta)
    if dec.corrected is not None:
        cw = dec.corrected
        cw_w = linalg.popcount(cw)
    else:
        cands = dec.candidates if hasattr(dec, "candidates") else None
        if cands:
            cw = min(cands, key=lambda c: (linalg.popcount(c), c))
            cw_w = linalg.popcount(cw)
        else:
            cw, cw_w = None, None
    tax = (cw_w * TAX_PER_W) if cw_w is not None else None
    return {
        "delta_weight": linalg.popcount(delta),
        "coset_weight": dec.weight,
        "syndrome": dec.syndrome,
        "codeword_weight": cw_w,
        "tax": float(tax) if tax is not None else None,
        "ambiguous": (dec.weight == 4),
    }


def measure_task_trace(task_name: str, n_samples: int = 50, H: int = 4, W: int = 6) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """For each trace, record per-step cost. Aggregate by step label."""
    fam = TASKS[task_name]
    per_step_costs: Dict[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]] = {}
    trace_lengths = []
    for i in range(n_samples):
        x, y, trace = fam.generate(seed=i + 1, H=H, W=W, dx=1, dy=1)
        trace_lengths.append(len(trace))
        prev = grid_to_bits_24(x)
        for label, grid in trace:
            if grid.size != 24:
                continue
            gb = grid_to_bits_24(grid)
            sc = step_cost(prev, gb)
            per_step_costs.setdefault(label, []).append(sc)
            prev = gb
    # Aggregate per step
    summary = {}
    for label, costs in per_step_costs.items():
        deltas = [c["delta_weight"] for c in costs]
        cosets = [c["coset_weight"] for c in costs]
        taxes  = [c["tax"] for c in costs if c["tax"] is not None]
        ambig  = [c["ambiguous"] for c in costs]
        cw_ws  = [c["codeword_weight"] for c in costs if c["codeword_weight"] is not None]
        summary[label] = {
            "n_samples": len(costs),
            "delta_weight_mean": float(np.mean(deltas)),
            "coset_weight_mean": float(np.mean(cosets)),
            "coset_weight_histogram": {int(k): int(v) for k, v in
                                         zip(*np.unique(cosets, return_counts=True))},
            "tax_mean": float(np.mean(taxes)) if taxes else None,
            "ambiguous_rate": float(np.mean(ambig)),
            "codeword_weight_mean": float(np.mean(cw_ws)) if cw_ws else None,
            "codeword_weight_histogram": {int(k): int(v) for k, v in
                                          zip(*np.unique(cw_ws, return_counts=True))} if cw_ws else {},
        }
    return {
        "task": task_name,
        "n_samples": n_samples,
        "trace_length_mean": float(np.mean(trace_lengths)),
        "trace_length_min": int(np.min(trace_lengths)),
        "trace_length_max": int(np.max(trace_lengths)),
        "per_step": summary,
    }


def render(results: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    lines = []
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("ITERATION 2 / PHASE 4 — Per-step cost in multi-step traces")
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("For each task with a multi-step trace, measure the per-step F_2 cost")
    lines.append("(syndrome, coset weight), codeword weight, and TAX. Identify decision")
    lines.append("points where the cost spikes — these are the escalation triggers the GLM")
    lines.append("must learn to handle.")
    lines.append("")
    for name, r in results.items():
        lines.append(f"--- {name} (trace length mean = {r['trace_length_mean']:.1f}, "
                     f"min = {r['trace_length_min']}, max = {r['trace_length_max']}) ---")
        lines.append(f"{'Step':<24}{'Δ wt':<10}{'coset wt':<14}{'TAX':<10}{'ambig%':<10}{'cw wt':<10}")
        for step_label, s in r["per_step"].items():
            tax_str = f"{s['tax_mean']:.3f}" if s['tax_mean'] is not None else "N/A"
            cw_str  = f"{s['codeword_weight_mean']:.1f}" if s['codeword_weight_mean'] is not None else "N/A"
            lines.append(f"{step_label:<24}"
                         f"{s['delta_weight_mean']:<10.2f}"
                         f"{s['coset_weight_mean']:<14.2f}"
                         f"{tax_str:<10}"
                         f"{100*s['ambiguous_rate']:<10.1f}"
                         f"{cw_str:<10}")
        lines.append("")
    lines.append("Interpretation:")
    lines.append("  * Steps where coset_weight = 4 are ESCALATION TRIGGERS: the substrate")
    lines.append("    cannot decide which octad to credit, and must lift to a higher lattice.")
    lines.append("  * Steps where codeword_weight > 0 (with coset_weight = 0) are CODEWORD")
    lines.append("    OPERATIONS: the displacement IS a codeword, and the substrate can")
    lines.append("    execute it exactly. The cost is the codeword's TAX.")
    lines.append("  * Steps where coset_weight ∈ {1, 2, 3} are WELL-DEFINED but require")
    lines.append("    syndrome decoding — the substrate handles them, but the operation is")
    lines.append("    not a pure codeword move.")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def main():
    os.makedirs("/home/z/my-project/data", exist_ok=True)
    print("Phase 4: per-step cost analysis (50 samples per task)")
    results = {}
    for name in ["toggle", "translate", "reflect_v", "flood_fill", "bfs_shortest"]:
        print(f"  {name}...", end=" ", flush=True)
        results[name] = measure_task_trace(name, n_samples=50)
        print(f"trace length mean = {results[name]['trace_length_mean']:.1f}")
    txt = render(results)
    with open(OUT_TXT, "w") as f:
        f.write(txt)
    with open(OUT_JSON, "w") as f:
        json.dump(results, f, indent=2, default=str)
    print()
    print(txt)
    print(f"\nWrote: {OUT_JSON}")
    print(f"Wrote: {OUT_TXT}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

iter2_05_plots.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Generate plots for iteration 2.

Outputs (in /home/z/my-project/download/iter2_plots/):
  cost_atlas_bars.png        — Phase 1: per-task mean cost (delta wt, coset wt, TAX)
  coset_weight_heatmap.png  — Phase 1: coset weight histogram per task
  wobble_signatures.png     — Phase 2: constants vs tasks wobble signatures
  hull_separation.png       — Phase 2: hull separation rate (constants vs tasks)
  leech_vs_golay_wobble.png — Phase 3: Leech vs Golay wobble (constants baseline)
  per_step_costs.png        — Phase 4: per-step coset weight + TAX
  escalation_ladder.png     — Summary: layered perspective (Golay -> Leech -> Barnes-Wall)
"""

import os, json
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.font_manager as fm
try:
    fm.fontManager.addfont('/usr/share/fonts/truetype/chinese/NotoSansSC[wght].ttf')
except Exception:
    pass
try:
    fm.fontManager.addfont('/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSans.ttf')
except Exception:
    pass
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
plt.rcParams['font.sans-serif'] = ['Noto Sans SC', 'DejaVu Sans']
plt.rcParams['axes.unicode_minus'] = False
import numpy as np

DATA = "/home/z/my-project/data"
OUT  = "/home/z/my-project/download/iter2_plots"
os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)


# ============================================================================
# Plot 1: Cost atlas bars (Phase 1)
# ============================================================================
def plot_cost_atlas():
    with open(f"{DATA}/iter2_phase1_atlas.json") as f:
        d = json.load(f)
    tasks = ["toggle", "translate", "reflect_v", "flood_fill", "bfs_shortest"]
    delta_means = [d[t]["delta_weight_mean"] for t in tasks]
    coset_means = [d[t]["coset_weight_mean"] for t in tasks]
    tax_means   = [d[t]["tax_mean"] for t in tasks]
    ambig_rates = [100 * d[t]["ambiguous_rate"] for t in tasks]
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(13, 4.5), constrained_layout=True)
    x = np.arange(len(tasks))
    w = 0.35
    # Left: delta weight + coset weight
    ax = axes[0]
    ax.bar(x - w/2, delta_means, w, label="Δ weight (bits changed)",
           color="#3498db", edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.bar(x + w/2, coset_means, w, label="coset weight (F_2 cost)",
           color="#e67e22", edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.set_xticks(x); ax.set_xticklabels(tasks, rotation=15)
    ax.set_ylabel("Mean weight")
    ax.set_ylim(0, 26)
    ax.set_title("Phase 1: Displacement weight vs coset weight")
    ax.legend(loc="upper right")
    ax.grid(axis="y", linestyle=":", alpha=0.4)
    # Right: TAX + ambiguous rate
    ax = axes[1]
    ax.bar(x - w/2, tax_means, w, label="TAX (codeword energy)",
           color="#9b59b6", edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.bar(x + w/2, ambig_rates, w, label="ambiguous % (coset wt=4)",
           color="#c0392b", edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.set_xticks(x); ax.set_xticklabels(tasks, rotation=15)
    ax.set_ylabel("TAX (left) / Ambiguous % (right)")
    ax.set_ylim(0, 105)
    ax.set_title("Phase 1: TAX cost + escalation trigger rate")
    ax.legend(loc="upper right")
    ax.grid(axis="y", linestyle=":", alpha=0.4)
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/cost_atlas_bars.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/cost_atlas_bars.png")


# ============================================================================
# Plot 2: Coset weight heatmap (Phase 1)
# ============================================================================
def plot_coset_heatmap():
    with open(f"{DATA}/iter2_phase1_atlas.json") as f:
        d = json.load(f)
    tasks = ["toggle", "translate", "reflect_v", "flood_fill", "bfs_shortest"]
    # Build a (5 x 5) matrix: task x coset weight (0..4)
    M = np.zeros((len(tasks), 5))
    for i, t in enumerate(tasks):
        hist = d[t]["coset_weight_histogram"]
        for k, v in hist.items():
            M[i, int(k)] = v
    # Normalise each row
    M_norm = M / M.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(8, 4.5), constrained_layout=True)
    im = ax.imshow(M_norm, cmap="YlOrRd", aspect="auto", vmin=0, vmax=1)
    ax.set_xticks(range(5)); ax.set_xticklabels([f"coset wt {i}" for i in range(5)])
    ax.set_yticks(range(len(tasks))); ax.set_yticklabels(tasks)
    ax.set_title("Phase 1: coset weight distribution per task (row-normalised)")
    # Annotate
    for i in range(len(tasks)):
        for j in range(5):
            val = M_norm[i, j]
            if val > 0:
                ax.text(j, i, f"{val*100:.0f}%", ha="center", va="center",
                         color="white" if val > 0.5 else "black",
                         fontsize=10, fontweight="bold")
    fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax, label="fraction of transitions")
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/coset_weight_heatmap.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/coset_weight_heatmap.png")


# ============================================================================
# Plot 3: Wobble signatures (Phase 2) — constants vs tasks
# ============================================================================
def plot_wobble_signatures():
    with open(f"{DATA}/iter2_phase2_wobble.json") as f:
        d = json.load(f)
    constants = list(d["constants"].keys())
    tasks = list(d["tasks"].keys())
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(13, 5), constrained_layout=True)
    # Left: constants baseline
    ax = axes[0]
    x = np.arange(len(constants))
    uniq = [d["constants"][c]["wobble"]["unique_codewords"] for c in constants]
    acc  = [d["constants"][c]["wobble"]["max_accumulator"] for c in constants]
    share = [d["constants"][c]["wobble"]["most_visited_share"] for c in constants]
    ax.bar(x - 0.2, uniq, 0.4, label="unique codewords", color="#3498db",
            edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax2 = ax.twinx()
    ax2.bar(x + 0.2, acc, 0.4, label="max accumulator", color="#c0392b",
              edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.set_xticks(x); ax.set_xticklabels(constants, rotation=20)
    ax.set_ylabel("unique codewords", color="#3498db")
    ax2.set_ylabel("max accumulator (linear growth = unreachable)", color="#c0392b")
    ax.set_title("Phase 2: Wobble on constants (Golay alphabet)")
    ax.set_ylim(0, 5)
    ax2.set_ylim(0, 5)
    ax.legend(loc="upper left"); ax2.legend(loc="upper right")
    # Right: tasks
    ax = axes[1]
    x = np.arange(len(tasks))
    uniq_t = [d["tasks"][t]["per_seed_summary"]["unique_codewords_mean"] for t in tasks]
    acc_t  = [d["tasks"][t]["per_seed_summary"]["max_accumulator_mean"] for t in tasks]
    sep_t  = [100 * d["tasks"][t]["per_seed_summary"]["hull_separates_rate"] for t in tasks]
    ax.bar(x - 0.2, uniq_t, 0.4, label="unique codewords", color="#3498db",
            edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax2 = ax.twinx()
    ax2.bar(x + 0.2, acc_t, 0.4, label="max accumulator", color="#c0392b",
              edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.set_xticks(x); ax.set_xticklabels(tasks, rotation=15)
    ax.set_ylabel("unique codewords", color="#3498db")
    ax2.set_ylabel("max accumulator (linear growth = unreachable)", color="#c0392b")
    ax.set_title("Phase 2: Wobble on task targets (Golay alphabet)")
    ax.set_ylim(0, 5)
    ax2.set_ylim(0, 450)
    ax.legend(loc="upper left"); ax2.legend(loc="upper right")
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/wobble_signatures.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/wobble_signatures.png")


# ============================================================================
# Plot 4: Hull separation rate (Phase 2)
# ============================================================================
def plot_hull_separation():
    with open(f"{DATA}/iter2_phase2_wobble.json") as f:
        d = json.load(f)
    constants = list(d["constants"].keys())
    tasks = list(d["tasks"].keys())
    const_sep = [100 if d["constants"][c]["hull"]["separates"] else 0 for c in constants]
    task_sep  = [100 * d["tasks"][t]["per_seed_summary"]["hull_separates_rate"] for t in tasks]
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(11, 4.5), constrained_layout=True)
    x = np.arange(len(constants) + len(tasks))
    vals = const_sep + task_sep
    colors = ["#27ae60"] * len(constants) + ["#c0392b"] * len(tasks)
    ax.bar(x, vals, color=colors, edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    labels = [f"const:\n{c}" for c in constants] + [f"task:\n{t}" for t in tasks]
    ax.set_xticks(x); ax.set_xticklabels(labels, rotation=0, fontsize=8)
    ax.set_ylabel("Hull-separation rate (%)")
    ax.set_ylim(0, 110)
    ax.set_title("Phase 2: hull certificate — is the target OUTSIDE the convex hull of the 4096 Golay codewords?\n"
                  "Green = inside (reachable by wobble); Red = outside (substrate MUST escalate)")
    # Threshold line
    ax.axhline(50, color="gray", linestyle="--", alpha=0.5)
    # Annotate
    for xi, v in zip(x, vals):
        ax.text(xi, v + 3, f"{v:.0f}%", ha="center", fontsize=9)
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/hull_separation.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/hull_separation.png")


# ============================================================================
# Plot 5: Leech vs Golay wobble (Phase 3)
# ============================================================================
def plot_leech_vs_golay():
    with open(f"{DATA}/iter2_phase2_wobble.json") as f:
        d2 = json.load(f)
    with open(f"{DATA}/iter2_phase3_leech.json") as f:
        d3 = json.load(f)
    constants = list(d2["constants"].keys())
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(11, 5), constrained_layout=True)
    x = np.arange(len(constants))
    w = 0.35
    # Golay acc_max (constants)
    golay_acc = [d2["constants"][c]["wobble"]["max_accumulator"] for c in constants]
    # Leech acc_max (constants)
    leech_acc = [d3["constants"][c]["max_accumulator"] for c in constants]
    ax.bar(x - w/2, golay_acc, w, label="Golay alphabet (4,096 codewords)",
           color="#3498db", edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.bar(x + w/2, leech_acc, w, label="Leech shell alphabet (196,560 minimal vectors)",
           color="#e67e22", edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.set_xticks(x); ax.set_xticklabels(constants, rotation=20)
    ax.set_ylabel("Max accumulator (lower = better convergence)")
    ax.set_ylim(0, 800)
    ax.set_title("Phase 3: Golay vs Leech wobble — constants baseline\n"
                  "sqrt2_minus_1 and phi_minus_1 CONVERGE on the Leech shell; others still fail")
    ax.legend(loc="upper left")
    ax.grid(axis="y", linestyle=":", alpha=0.4)
    # Annotate convergence (acc < 10)
    for xi, v in zip(x, leech_acc):
        if v < 10:
            ax.text(xi + w/2, v + 15, "✓ converges", ha="center",
                     color="#27ae60", fontsize=9, fontweight="bold")
        elif v < 100:
            ax.text(xi + w/2, v + 15, "partial", ha="center",
                     color="#a68544", fontsize=9)
        else:
            ax.text(xi + w/2, v + 15, "✗ diverges", ha="center",
                     color="#c0392b", fontsize=9)
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/leech_vs_golay_wobble.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/leech_vs_golay_wobble.png")


# ============================================================================
# Plot 6: Per-step costs (Phase 4)
# ============================================================================
def plot_per_step_costs():
    with open(f"{DATA}/iter2_phase4_per_step.json") as f:
        d = json.load(f)
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 3, figsize=(15, 8), constrained_layout=True)
    axes = axes.flatten()
    task_colors = {"toggle": "#3498db", "translate": "#e67e22",
                     "reflect_v": "#9b59b6", "flood_fill": "#27ae60",
                     "bfs_shortest": "#c0392b"}
    for i, (name, r) in enumerate(d.items()):
        ax = axes[i]
        steps = list(r["per_step"].keys())
        cosets = [r["per_step"][s]["coset_weight_mean"] for s in steps]
        taxes  = [r["per_step"][s]["tax_mean"] for s in steps]
        ambig  = [100 * r["per_step"][s]["ambiguous_rate"] for s in steps]
        x = np.arange(len(steps))
        w = 0.3
        ax.bar(x - w, cosets, w, label="coset wt", color=task_colors[name],
                edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5, alpha=0.7)
        ax.bar(x, taxes, w, label="TAX", color="#7f8c8d",
                edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5, alpha=0.7)
        ax.bar(x + w, [a / 25 for a in ambig], w, label="ambig % (÷25)",
                color="#c0392b", edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5, alpha=0.7)
        ax.set_xticks(x); ax.set_xticklabels(steps, rotation=20, fontsize=8)
        ax.set_ylim(0, 11)
        ax.set_title(f"{name}", fontsize=11)
        ax.set_ylabel("cost")
        ax.grid(axis="y", linestyle=":", alpha=0.4)
        if i == 0:
            ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=8)
        # Mark escalation zones (coset wt = 4)
        for xi, c in zip(x, cosets):
            if c >= 3.5:
                ax.axvspan(xi - 0.5, xi + 0.5, color="red", alpha=0.08)
                ax.text(xi, 10, "ESCALATE", ha="center", color="red",
                         fontsize=8, fontweight="bold")
    axes[5].axis("off")
    axes[5].text(0.5, 0.5, "Red shaded zones = steps where coset wt ≈ 4\n"
                            "(deep hole — substrate cannot decide octad;\n"
                            "must escalate to Leech lattice)",
                  ha="center", va="center", fontsize=10, transform=axes[5].transAxes)
    fig.suptitle("Phase 4: per-step cost in multi-step transformation traces",
                   fontsize=13)
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/per_step_costs.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/per_step_costs.png")


# ============================================================================
# Plot 7: Escalation ladder (summary)
# ============================================================================
def plot_escalation_ladder():
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(12, 6), constrained_layout=True)
    ax.axis("off")
    layers = [
        ("F_2^24 / G_24\n(binary quotient)\n4,096 cosets\nTAX range: 0 to ~9.35",
         "#34495e", 0.05),
        ("Golay code\n[24,12,8]\n759 octads\nS(5,8,24), min TAX = 3.117",
         "#16a085", 0.22),
        ("Leech lattice Λ_24\n196,560 minimal vectors\nnorm² 32, no roots\nconvex hull = bounded ball",
         "#8e44ad", 0.42),
        ("Barnes-Wall Λ_32\n146,880 minimal vectors\nthree nested resolutions\n(4Z^32 ⊂ 2·RM(3,5) ⊂ RM(1,5))",
         "#c0392b", 0.65),
        ("Ternary Λ_48\n96 words of norm² 18\n16,834× denser than Leech\n(no Golay; uses F_3 code)",
         "#7f8c8d", 0.88),
    ]
    for label, color, x in layers:
        ax.text(x, 0.55, label, ha="center", va="center", fontsize=9,
                bbox=dict(boxstyle="round,pad=0.6", facecolor=color, edgecolor="black", alpha=0.85),
                color="white", fontweight="bold")
    # Arrows
    for i in range(len(layers) - 1):
        x1 = layers[i][2] + 0.06
        x2 = layers[i+1][2] - 0.06
        ax.annotate("", xy=(x2, 0.55), xytext=(x1, 0.55),
                    arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle="->", lw=2, color="#7f8c8d"))
    # Failure annotations between layers
    failures = [
        (0.13, "Phase 1: toggle Δ is a codeword\n(cw wt 24); F_2 quotient loses it."),
        (0.32, "Phase 2: ALL task targets lie OUTSIDE\nthe Golay hull (hull_sep = 100%)."),
        (0.53, "Phase 3: binary targets in ±4 encoding\nlie outside the Leech shell hull too."),
        (0.76, "Open: does Λ_32's three nested resolutions\nresolve the deep-hole ambiguity?"),
    ]
    for x, text in failures:
        ax.text(x, 0.20, text, ha="center", va="top", fontsize=8.5,
                color="#c0392b", style="italic",
                bbox=dict(boxstyle="round,pad=0.3", facecolor="#fff5f5",
                           edgecolor="#c0392b", alpha=0.9))
    ax.text(0.5, 0.92, "Layered perspective: failure indicates the required mechanism for progression up the ladder",
            ha="center", va="top", fontsize=12, fontweight="bold")
    ax.set_xlim(0, 1); ax.set_ylim(0, 1)
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/escalation_ladder.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/escalation_ladder.png")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    plot_cost_atlas()
    plot_coset_heatmap()
    plot_wobble_signatures()
    plot_hull_separation()
    plot_leech_vs_golay()
    plot_per_step_costs()
    plot_escalation_ladder()
    print("\nAll plots written to", OUT)

iter2_06_report.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Generate the Iteration 2 PDF report.

Builds on Iteration 1 by adding:
  - Phase 1: Golay-syndrome cost atlas (Golay as measuring device)
  - Phase 2: Delta-sigma wobble signatures + hull certificate
  - Phase 3: Leech native 24-D wobble (full 196,560 minimal vectors)
  - Phase 4: Per-step cost in multi-step traces (escalation triggers)
  - Summary: layered perspective (Golay -> Leech -> Barnes-Wall)
"""

import os, sys
from reportlab.lib import colors
from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import A4
from reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle
from reportlab.lib.units import cm, mm
from reportlab.lib.enums import TA_LEFT, TA_CENTER
from reportlab.pdfbase import pdfmetrics
from reportlab.pdfbase.ttfonts import TTFont
from reportlab.platypus import (
    SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, Image, PageBreak,
    Table, TableStyle, KeepTogether
)

pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('BodySerif',   '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/noto-serif-sc/NotoSerifSC-Regular.ttf'))
pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('BodySerifB', '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/noto-serif-sc/NotoSerifSC-Bold.ttf'))
pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('BodySans',    '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/noto-serif-sc/NotoSerifSC-Medium.ttf'))
pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('Mono',        '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSansMono.ttf'))

# Palette (from palette.cascade, dark mode for an "iteration" feel)
PAGE_BG       = colors.HexColor('#f1f0ef')
SECTION_BG    = colors.HexColor('#f1f0ee')
CARD_BG       = colors.HexColor('#ebeae5')
TABLE_STRIPE  = colors.HexColor('#ecebe9')
HEADER_FILL   = colors.HexColor('#5a4e2a')
COVER_BLOCK   = colors.HexColor('#4a3e1a')
BORDER        = colors.HexColor('#c9c5bb')
ACCENT        = colors.HexColor('#8a6f1c')
ACCENT_2      = colors.HexColor('#4ea3bf')
TEXT_PRIMARY  = colors.HexColor('#191816')
TEXT_MUTED    = colors.HexColor('#8c8a83')
SEM_SUCCESS   = colors.HexColor('#49835c')
SEM_WARNING   = colors.HexColor('#a68544')
SEM_ERROR     = colors.HexColor('#93504a')
SEM_INFO      = colors.HexColor('#4e769d')

OUT_PDF = "/home/z/my-project/download/GLM_Iteration2_Exact_Reasoning.pdf"
PLOT_DIR = "/home/z/my-project/download/iter2_plots"


def _draw_page(canvas, doc):
    canvas.saveState()
    w, h = A4
    canvas.setStrokeColor(BORDER); canvas.setLineWidth(0.5)
    canvas.line(2*cm, h - 1.5*cm, w - 2*cm, h - 1.5*cm)
    canvas.setFont('BodySerif', 8); canvas.setFillColor(TEXT_MUTED)
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, 1.2*cm, "GLM Iteration 2 — Exact Reasoning via the Layered Perspective")
    canvas.drawRightString(w - 2*cm, 1.2*cm, f"Page {canvas.getPageNumber()}")
    canvas.restoreState()


def _draw_cover(canvas, doc):
    canvas.saveState()
    w, h = A4
    # Top color block
    canvas.setFillColor(COVER_BLOCK)
    canvas.rect(0, h - 14*cm, w, 14*cm, fill=1, stroke=0)
    canvas.setFillColor(ACCENT)
    canvas.rect(0, h - 14*cm - 0.3*cm, w, 0.3*cm, fill=1, stroke=0)
    # Title
    canvas.setFillColor(colors.white)
    canvas.setFont('BodySerifB', 28)
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, h - 5.5*cm, "GLM Iteration 2")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, h - 7*cm,   "Exact Reasoning via the")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, h - 8.5*cm, "Layered Perspective")
    canvas.setFont('BodySans', 12)
    canvas.setFillColor(colors.HexColor('#e8e6df'))
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, h - 9.6*cm,
                       "What does it take to get this substrate to reason exactly?")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, h - 10.4*cm,
                       "Golay as measuring device, wobble path shapes, escalation triggers.")
    # Metadata
    canvas.setFillColor(TEXT_PRIMARY); canvas.setFont('BodySerifB', 11)
    y0 = h - 16*cm
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0, "Phases")
    canvas.setFont('BodySerif', 10); canvas.setFillColor(TEXT_MUTED)
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 0.5*cm, "  P1 Golay-syndrome cost atlas (Golay as measuring device)")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 0.9*cm, "  P2 Delta-sigma wobble + hull certificate")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 1.3*cm, "  P3 Leech native 24-D wobble (full 196,560 minimal vectors)")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 1.7*cm, "  P4 Per-step cost analysis (escalation triggers in multi-step traces)")
    canvas.setFillColor(TEXT_PRIMARY); canvas.setFont('BodySerifB', 11)
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 2.6*cm, "Headline finding")
    canvas.setFont('BodySerif', 10); canvas.setFillColor(TEXT_MUTED)
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 3.1*cm,
                       "  Every binary task target lies OUTSIDE the convex hull of the 4,096 Golay")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 3.5*cm,
                       "  codewords (Phase 2). Translate's mask_block and erase_old steps sit at")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 3.9*cm,
                       "  deep holes (coset wt = 4) in 100% of trials (Phase 4) — the substrate")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 4.3*cm,
                       "  mathematically cannot decide which octad to credit. This is the layered")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 4.7*cm,
                       "  perspective in action: failure indicates the required mechanism for")
    canvas.drawString(2*cm, y0 - 5.1*cm,
                       "  progression up the ladder (Golay -> Leech -> Barnes-Wall).")
    canvas.restoreState()


def make_styles():
    ss = getSampleStyleSheet()
    body = ParagraphStyle('Body', parent=ss['BodyText'],
                           fontName='BodySerif', fontSize=10.5, leading=15.5,
                           alignment=TA_LEFT, textColor=TEXT_PRIMARY, spaceAfter=8)
    h1 = ParagraphStyle('H1', parent=ss['Heading1'], fontName='BodySerifB',
                          fontSize=18, leading=22, textColor=HEADER_FILL,
                          spaceBefore=14, spaceAfter=10, keepWithNext=1)
    h2 = ParagraphStyle('H2', parent=ss['Heading2'], fontName='BodySerifB',
                          fontSize=13, leading=17, textColor=ACCENT,
                          spaceBefore=10, spaceAfter=6, keepWithNext=1)
    h3 = ParagraphStyle('H3', parent=ss['Heading3'], fontName='BodySerifB',
                          fontSize=11, leading=14, textColor=TEXT_PRIMARY,
                          spaceBefore=6, spaceAfter=4, keepWithNext=1)
    callout = ParagraphStyle('Callout', parent=body, fontName='BodySerifB',
                              fontSize=10.5, textColor=ACCENT_2,
                              leftIndent=10, spaceBefore=4, spaceAfter=10)
    return body, h1, h2, h3, callout


def build_story():
    body, h1, h2, h3, callout = make_styles()
    S = lambda h: Spacer(1, h)
    story = []
    story.append(PageBreak())  # body starts on page 2 (cover on page 1)

    # ====== Section 1: Executive Summary ======
    story.append(Paragraph("1.  Executive Summary", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Iteration 1 established that the F<sub>2</sub> binary quotient of the "
        "Golay code adds <b>no transfer power</b> beyond an arbitrary linear "
        "projection (249/250 identical trials). Iteration 2 asks the deeper "
        "question the user posed: <i>what does it take to get this substrate "
        "to reason exactly?</i> The user's clarifications mapped directly onto "
        "the GLM academic paper's existing machinery — the delta-sigma "
        "modulator (the 'wobble'), the hull certificate, and the layered "
        "perspective where each layer's failure indicates the required "
        "mechanism for progression up the ladder.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "We ran four phases against the actual GLM substrate. Phase 1 measures "
        "the Golay syndrome and TAX cost of every binary transition (Golay as a "
        "measuring device, per clarification a). Phase 2 runs the substrate's "
        "<font name='Mono' size='9'>exact_real.golay_delta_sigma</font> wobble on each "
        "task target and uses the <font name='Mono' size='9'>hull_certificate</font> "
        "to test reachability. Phase 3 implements a Leech native 24-D wobble "
        "using the full 196,560 minimal vectors. Phase 4 measures per-step "
        "cost in multi-step transformation traces to identify escalation "
        "triggers.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("Headline findings:", h3))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "1. <b>Phase 1 (Golay as measuring device):</b> each task family has a "
        "characteristic syndrome signature. Toggle has coset wt = 0 (the "
        "displacement IS a codeword) but codeword wt = 24 (TAX = 9.35, the "
        "highest). Translate has coset wt = 4 in 100% of trials — every "
        "transition is at a deep hole, the substrate cannot decide which octad "
        "to credit. Reflect_v is 79% at deep holes; flood_fill and "
        "bfs_shortest are well-defined (0% ambiguous).", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "2. <b>Phase 2 (Golay wobble + hull certificate):</b> ALL 5 task family "
        "query grids lie <b>OUTSIDE the convex hull of the 4,096 Golay "
        "codewords</b> (100% hull-separation rate). The substrate "
        "mathematically cannot reach any of them via the Golay delta-sigma. "
        "The accumulator grows linearly at the predicted rate of ~1 per tick. "
        "Meanwhile, the constants (√2, π, e, 1/3, φ) all lie INSIDE the hull "
        "and the wobble tracks them with bounded accumulator and within-1/N "
        "deviation.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "3. <b>Phase 3 (Leech native 24-D wobble):</b> against the full 196,560 "
        "minimal vectors in R<sup>24</sup>, the constants √2 − 1 and φ − 1 "
        "CONVERGE (acc_max bounded at 5.67 and 26.19 respectively, deviation "
        "drops to 0.0063 for √2). But the binary task targets (encoded as ±4 "
        "per coordinate) all DIVERGE — the targets are outside the Leech shell "
        "hull too. This is the layered perspective: the Leech shell escalates "
        "the constants that the Golay alphabet handled, but binary grids need "
        "a further lift (the 32-D Barnes-Wall lattice, or proper Leech lattice "
        "points, not just the minimal-vector shell).", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "4. <b>Phase 4 (per-step cost analysis):</b> the per-step breakdown "
        "shows WHERE the substrate fails. For translate, 2 of the 4 steps "
        "(mask_block and erase_old) sit at deep holes in 100% of trials — "
        "these are explicit escalation triggers. For toggle, the bitwise_not "
        "step is a CODEWORD operation (executable exactly at high TAX). For "
        "flood_fill and bfs_shortest, every step is well-defined.", body))
    story.append(S(6))

    # ====== Section 2: Phase 1 — Golay as Measuring Device ======
    story.append(Paragraph("2.  Phase 1 — Golay as measuring device", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Per the user's clarification (a): <i>the Golay code isn't just for "
        "error correction, when we don't want to change data we use it as a "
        "measuring device to see the cost and inner working of any given data "
        "transaction.</i> For each transition x → y on a 4×6 binary grid (24 "
        "bits), we measure three quantities:", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "&#8226; <b>Δ weight</b>: the Hamming weight of x &#8853; y — the number of cells "
        "that change. This is a content-blind count.<br/>"
        "&#8226; <b>coset weight</b>: the syndrome of x &#8853; y in F<sub>2</sub><sup>24</sup> / "
        "G<sub>24</sub>, in {0, 1, 2, 3, 4}. This is the F<sub>2</sub> cost of the "
        "transition. coset wt = 0 means the displacement IS a codeword (the F<sub>2</sub> "
        "quotient sees it as a no-op). coset wt = 4 means a deep hole (sextet "
        "ambiguity — the substrate cannot decide which octad to credit).<br/>"
        "&#8226; <b>TAX</b>: w × (Y + 1/8) where Y = 1/(π + 2/π) ≈ 0.2647 and w is the "
        "Hamming weight of the nearest codeword to the displacement. The minimum "
        "non-zero TAX is at octads (w = 8), giving 8Y + 1 = 3.1174.", body))

    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/cost_atlas_bars.png")
    img.drawWidth = 16.5*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (4.5/13.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 1.</i> Per-task cost atlas. Left: mean Δ weight (bits changed) vs "
        "mean coset weight (F<sub>2</sub> cost). Right: TAX (codeword energy) and "
        "ambiguous-rate (fraction of transitions at deep holes). Toggle has the "
        "highest TAX (codeword wt 24); translate is 100% ambiguous; flood_fill "
        "and bfs_shortest are well-defined.", body))

    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/coset_weight_heatmap.png")
    img.drawWidth = 13*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (4.5/8.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 2.</i> Coset weight distribution per task (row-normalised). Toggle "
        "is 100% at coset wt 0 (the all-ones codeword displacement). Translate is "
        "100% at coset wt 4 (deep hole). Reflect_v is 79% at wt 4 + 19% at wt 2. "
        "Flood_fill spans wts 1-3. BFS_shortest spans wts 0-4.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>Interpretation.</b> The cost atlas reveals three distinct substrate-"
        "level failure modes. (1) <b>Toggle is information-free at the F<sub>2</sub> "
        "quotient</b>: its displacement IS a codeword, so the F<sub>2</sub> quotient "
        "sees the operation as a no-op (this is exactly why Iteration 1's "
        "Golay-coset solver failed on toggle). But the codeword weight is 24, "
        "the highest possible, so the operation carries high TAX — the substrate "
        "would have to lift to the codeword-weight layer to see the operation. "
        "(2) <b>Translate is fundamentally ambiguous</b>: every transition sits at "
        "a deep hole, where 6 codewords are equidistant. The substrate cannot "
        "decide which octad to credit; this is a forced escalation trigger. "
        "(3) <b>Flood_fill and BFS_shortest are well-defined</b>: the substrate "
        "handles them via syndrome decoding with no ambiguity.", body))

    story.append(PageBreak())

    # ====== Section 3: Phase 2 — Wobble signatures + hull certificate ======
    story.append(Paragraph("3.  Phase 2 — Delta-sigma wobble + hull certificate", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Per the user's clarification (b): <i>looking simply at the difference "
        "between any two data changes is not enough, we also need to look at "
        "the path it takes, not just the start/end points or nodes - what is "
        "the exact shape of this 'decision'.</i> The GLM academic paper (§5.2-5.3) "
        "introduces the delta-sigma modulator — a deterministic error-feedback "
        "loop where an accumulator integrates (target − emitted), the quantiser "
        "(Golay snap) forces the value back to the nearest codeword, and the "
        "running mean of emitted codewords converges to the target at rate 1/N. "
        "The trajectory of codewords visited IS the path shape of the decision.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("3.1  Constants baseline", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "We first ran the wobble on six known constants (each placed in all 24 "
        "coordinates) as a reference. The GLM paper reports these have distinct "
        "vibrational signatures (§5.4). The substrate confirms: √2−1 visits 2 "
        "codewords with share 0.585 (a quasiperiodic alternation); π−3 visits 2 "
        "codewords with share 0.858 (a more lopsided alternation); 1/3 has share "
        "0.667; e−2 has share 0.718; φ−1 has share 0.618 (the golden ratio "
        "in the orbit itself).", body))

    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/wobble_signatures.png")
    img.drawWidth = 16*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (5/13.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 3.</i> Wobble signatures on constants (left) and task targets "
        "(right). Constants visit ~2 codewords with bounded accumulator (sublinear "
        "growth, the wobble tracks them). Task targets visit 1 codeword with "
        "linearly-growing accumulator — the wobble is stuck.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("3.2  Hull certificate", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "The substrate's <font name='Mono' size='9'>hull_certificate</font> runs the "
        "wobble with the minimum-norm rule, takes the accumulator's drift "
        "direction as a candidate separating functional c, and verifies exactly "
        "against all 4,096 codewords: if &#10216;c, target&#10217; exceeds "
        "&#10216;c, w&#10217; for every codeword w, the target is provably "
        "outside the convex hull and unreachable by ANY quantiser rule. This is "
        "an exact mathematical certificate, not a measurement.", body))

    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/hull_separation.png")
    img.drawWidth = 16*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (4.5/11.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 4.</i> Hull-separation rate. Green bars (constants) are inside "
        "the hull (reachable); red bars (task targets) are 100% outside. Every "
        "binary task target is provably unreachable by the Golay delta-sigma — "
        "the substrate must escalate.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>Verdict for Phase 2:</b> the binary task targets lie provably outside "
        "the Golay hull. The substrate CANNOT reach them with the 4,096-codeword "
        "alphabet. The accumulator grows linearly at exactly the rate the GLM "
        "paper's §8.4 'wall' theorem predicts. This is the layered perspective's "
        "first failure: the Golay layer cannot reason about binary "
        "transformations on grids; escalation to the Leech layer is required.", callout))

    story.append(PageBreak())

    # ====== Section 4: Phase 3 — Leech native 24-D wobble ======
    story.append(Paragraph("4.  Phase 3 — Leech native 24-D wobble", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Per the user's clarification (d): <i>the Leech in its native 24-D is "
        "the natural progression, according to GLM findings there is a layered "
        "perspective to almost all things where failure indicated the required "
        "mechanism for progression up the ladder.</i> Phase 2 showed that "
        "every binary task target lies outside the Golay hull. Phase 3 tests "
        "whether the Leech lattice's 196,560 minimal vectors — points in "
        "R<sup>24</sup>, not in F<sub>2</sub><sup>24</sup> — can reach them.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph(
        "We implemented the matched-rule delta-sigma (the GLM paper's Rule 2, "
        "§8.4): at each tick, emit the minimal vector v that maximises "
        "&#10216;v, accumulator + target&#10217; (the support function). Update "
        "the accumulator by subtracting v. The running mean of emitted vectors "
        "converges to the target iff the target is in the convex hull of the "
        "alphabet (a paper theorem, §8.4).", body))

    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/leech_vs_golay_wobble.png")
    img.drawWidth = 16*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (5/11.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<i>Figure 5.</i> Golay vs Leech wobble on constants baseline. √2−1 and "
        "φ−1 CONVERGE on the Leech shell (acc_max bounded at 5.67 and 26.19). "
        "Constants with target values close to ±4 (the alphabet's per-coordinate "
        "maximum) still diverge.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>Result for binary task targets:</b> all 5 task families diverge on the "
        "Leech shell alphabet (acc_max around 600-680 over 200 ticks). The "
        "bipolar ±4 encoding of binary grids places the target outside the "
        "Leech minimal-vector hull — the target has all 24 coordinates nonzero "
        "(norm² = 24 × 16 = 384, far exceeding the alphabet's 32), so no convex "
        "combination of alphabet vectors (each with at most 8 nonzero "
        "coordinates) can produce it.", body))

    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>Verdict for Phase 3:</b> the Leech shell alphabet (196,560 minimal "
        "vectors) escalates the constants that the Golay alphabet handled "
        "(√2 and φ converge here), but the binary task targets require a "
        "further lift. The next layers per the GLM paper are: (a) the full Leech "
        "lattice (all integer points, not just minimal vectors — but the "
        "Construction-C sieve requires substantial implementation); (b) the "
        "32-dimensional Barnes-Wall lattice Λ<sub>32</sub>, which has three "
        "nested resolutions (4Z<sup>32</sup> ⊂ 2·RM(3,5) ⊂ RM(1,5)) and can "
        "represent multi-resolution structures natively; (c) the 48-dimensional "
        "ternary lattice Λ<sub>48</sub>, which is 16,834× denser per unit cell "
        "but abandons the binary picture entirely.", callout))

    story.append(PageBreak())

    # ====== Section 5: Phase 4 — Per-step cost (escalation triggers) ======
    story.append(Paragraph("5.  Phase 4 — Per-step cost in multi-step traces", h1))
    story.append(Paragraph(

    img = Image(f"{PLOT_DIR}/per_step_costs.png")
    img.drawWidth = 17*cm
    img.drawHeight = img.drawWidth * (8/15.0)
    story.append(img)
    story.append(Paragraph(

    # Per-step summary table
    story.append(Paragraph("5.1  Per-task breakdown", h2))
    cell_style = ParagraphStyle('Cell', fontName='BodySerif', fontSize=8.5,
                                  leading=11, textColor=TEXT_PRIMARY)

        ["toggle", "bitwise_not",     "24.0", "0.0", "9.352", "0%",   "24"],

        "Three concrete follow-ups, in priority order:", body))

        "<b>1. Implement the full Leech lattice snap (Construction C).</b> The "

        "but the Leech lattice itself contains all integer combinations of "
        "those vectors, not just the minimal ones. The Construction-C sieve "

        "binary grid targets be encoded as proper Leech lattice points, "

        "also the syndrome, TAX, and hull-membership of the transition. The "
        "loss combines grid-BCE + cost-MSE. The hypothesis: a model that "
        "learns to predict the per-step cost will learn to <i>choose</i> "
        "the right lattice layer for each step, escalating when needed. This "
        "is the operational form of 'the GLM is taught the path shape, not "
        "just the endpoints'.", body))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "<b>3. Lift to the 32-D Barnes-Wall lattice Λ<sub>32</sub>.</b> The GLM "
        "academic paper (§8.2) describes Λ<sub>32</sub>'s three nested "
        "resolutions (4Z<sup>32</sup> ⊂ 2·RM(3,5) ⊂ RM(1,5)) as providing "
        "'three usable resolutions where Λ<sub>24</sub> provides one — a "
        "coarse address that is usable on its own and refinable later.' The "
        "binary task targets in our experiment may require this multi-"
        "resolution structure to be reachable. This is a substantially larger "
        "implementation effort (the substrate has <font name='Mono' size='9'>"
        "lattice32.py</font> with the construction, but no wobble against it).", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("7.2  What did NOT work", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "Two design choices that did not work and are worth flagging for the "
        "next iteration: (1) The first Phase 3 attempt used only the 1,104 "
        "sparse-shell minimal vectors (shape (±4², 0²²)) as the alphabet. "
        "This alphabet was too small — even constants like √2 failed to "
        "converge. Switching to the full 196,560 minimal vectors fixed the "
        "constants (√2 and φ now converge) but the binary task targets still "
        "fail because the ±4 encoding puts them outside the alphabet's per-"
        "coordinate range. (2) The bipolar ±4 encoding of binary grids was "
        "naive — each coordinate becomes ±4, but the Leech minimal vectors "
        "have at most 8 nonzero coordinates, so a target with all 24 nonzero "
        "is structurally outside the alphabet's hull. The right encoding is "
        "to map each binary grid to its nearest Leech lattice point via "
        "Construction C (which preserves the 24-bit information at the Leech "
        "level).", body))

    story.append(Paragraph("7.3  Limitations", h2))
    story.append(Paragraph(
        "All experiments were CPU-only on 4×6 binary grids. The Phase 2 "
        "hull-certificate result (100% separation for binary task targets) "
        "is exact and exhaustive — it's a mathematical certificate, not a "
        "measurement, so it cannot be disputed by sampling more. The Phase 3 "
        "Leech shell wobble used 200 ticks per target; longer runs would give "
        "tighter convergence bounds but would not change the qualitative "
        "result (constants converge, binary targets diverge). The Phase 4 "
        "per-step cost atlas is on 50 samples per task family — enough for "
        "the means and ambiguity rates to be statistically meaningful but "
        "not for fine-grained per-step distribution analysis. The next "
        "iteration's full-Leech-snap implementation (recommendation 1 above) "
        "is the highest-leverage follow-up: it would either confirm or refute "
        "the layered-progression hypothesis directly.", body))

    return story


def main():
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(OUT_PDF), exist_ok=True)
    doc = SimpleDocTemplate(OUT_PDF, pagesize=A4,
                              leftMargin=2*cm, rightMargin=2*cm,
                              topMargin=2*cm, bottomMargin=2*cm,
                              title="GLM Iteration 2 — Exact Reasoning via the Layered Perspective",
                              author="Z.ai",
                              subject="What does it take to get this substrate to reason exactly?",
                              creator="Z.ai")
    story = build_story()
    doc.build(story, onFirstPage=_draw_cover, onLaterPages=_draw_page)
    print(f"Wrote: {OUT_PDF}")
    print(f"Size: {os.path.getsize(OUT_PDF)/1024:.1f} KB")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


====

iter3_01_step_rules.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Iteration 3 — Step-rule library + Construction-C Leech snap as rule composition.

The user's insight: the step rules (popcount, Hamming, sphere-packing, octad
enumeration, theta series, pair census) are the GLM's learning primitives.
Each is a computational primitive at a specific layer of the
F_2 -> Hamming -> G_23 -> G_24 -> Lambda_24 -> Co_0 chain.

This module defines the 6 step rules as clean callables, then implements the
Construction-C Leech snap AS A COMPOSITION OF THESE RULES — showing which
rule is invoked at each stage of the snap.

The 6 step rules (one per layer):

  Layer 1: F_2     popcount(x)            count 1-bits in a 24-bit word
  Layer 2: Hamming hamming(x, y)          XOR-then-popcount (the metric)
  Layer 3: G_23    sphere_packing(n, k)    combinatorial sum check (C(n,k) sum)
  Layer 4: G_24    enumerate_octads()     list the 759 octads (Steiner S(5,8,24))
  Layer 5: Lambda   theta_coefficient(N)   theta series coefficient (E_4^3 - 720*Delta)
  Layer 6: Co_0     pair_census()          invariants over 98,280 type-2 axes
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import sys, os, json
from fractions import Fraction
from typing import Dict, List, Any, Tuple, Optional
import numpy as np
from math import comb

GLM_REPO = "/home/z/my-project/glm_repo"
sys.path.insert(0, GLM_REPO)
sys.path.insert(0, "/home/z/my-project/scripts")

import warnings
warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=SyntaxWarning)

from glm_universal.substrate import leech2, mog, golay_decode, linalg
from bin_tasks import TASKS

OUT_JSON = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter3_step_rules.json"
OUT_TXT  = "/home/z/my-project/data/iter3_step_rules.txt"


# ============================================================================
# The 6 step rules
# ============================================================================

class StepRule:
    """A named step rule with a layer, a cost (in substrate calls), and a callable."""
    def __init__(self, name: str, layer: str, callable_, cost: str):
        self.name = name
        self.layer = layer
        self.callable_ = callable_
        self.cost = cost
    def __call__(self, *args, **kwargs):
        return self.callable_(*args, **kwargs)
    def describe(self) -> str:
        return f"{self.name} [{self.layer}] cost={self.cost}"


def _popcount(bits: int) -> int:
    return linalg.popcount(bits)

def _hamming(x: int, y: int) -> int:
    return linalg.popcount(x ^ y)

def _sphere_packing_check(n: int, k: int) -> bool:
    """Check that sum_{i=0..k} C(n, i) = 2^(n - k_dim) where k_dim = n - log2(sum).
    For G_23 = [23,12,7]: sum_{i=0..3} C(23,i) = 1 + 23 + 253 + 1771 = 2048 = 2^11.
    """
    s = sum(comb(n, i) for i in range(k + 1))
    return s & (s - 1) == 0  # is s a power of 2?

def _enumerate_octads() -> List[int]:
    return list(mog.OCTAD_MASKS)

def _theta_coefficient(N: int) -> int:
    return leech2.theta_series(max(N + 1, 1))[N]

def _pair_census() -> Dict[int, int]:
    return dict(leech2.pair_census())


STEP_RULES = {
    "popcount":      StepRule("popcount",      "F_2",     _popcount,             "1 popcount call"),
    "hamming":       StepRule("hamming",       "Hamming", _hamming,              "1 XOR + 1 popcount"),
    "sphere_packing":StepRule("sphere_packing","G_23",    _sphere_packing_check, "1 combinatorial sum"),
    "octads":        StepRule("octads",        "G_24",    _enumerate_octads,     "759 octads (one-time)"),
    "theta":         StepRule("theta",         "Lambda",  _theta_coefficient,    "1 modular-form coefficient"),
    "pair_census":   StepRule("pair_census",   "Co_0",    _pair_census,          "exhaustive over 98,280 axes"),
}


# ============================================================================
# Construction-C Leech snap AS A COMPOSITION OF STEP RULES
# ============================================================================
#
# The snap takes a 24-D real target and rounds it to the nearest Leech lattice
# point. Construction C requires:
#   (mod 2) all coords even OR all coords odd
#   (mod 4) the set {i : x_i = 2 mod 4} (even) or {i : x_i = 3 mod 4} (odd)
#           is a Golay codeword support
#   (mod 8) sum(x) = 0 mod 8 (even coset) or 4 mod 8 (odd coset)
#
# Each step uses ONE STEP RULE:
#   - parity check: popcount (counts the 1s; for parity mod 2)
#   - Hamming distance to candidate Golay codewords: hamming
#   - sphere-packing check: confirms we're at a valid [n, k, d] code
#   - octad enumeration: lists the weight-8 codewords (one is used as the support)
#   - theta series: verifies the lattice level (optional sanity)
#   - pair census: not invoked here (used at the Co_0 layer, above Λ_24)

def snap_to_leech_with_trace(target_bits: int) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Snap a 24-bit binary target to a Leech lattice point, recording each step rule.

    Returns a dict with:
      'principal_candidate': 4*target_bits (if in Leech)
      'odd_candidate':       4*target_bits + glue (if in Leech, with chosen glue)
      'in_principal':       bool
      'in_odd':             bool
      'parity':             'even' or 'odd' (popcount of target_bits mod 2)
      'wall':               str or None (the wall hit, if any)
      'trace':              list of (step_rule_name, layer, input, output, note)
    """
    trace: List[Tuple[str, str, Any, Any, str]] = []
    n_ones = STEP_RULES["popcount"](target_bits)
    trace.append(("popcount", "F_2", target_bits, n_ones,
                   f"Number of 1-bits in the target word"))

    parity = "even" if n_ones % 2 == 0 else "odd"
    # The principal candidate: scale by 4 (each bit becomes 0 or 4)
    t_principal = tuple(4 * ((target_bits >> (23 - i)) & 1) for i in range(24))
    sum_p = sum(t_principal)
    trace.append(("sum_mod_8", "G_24 (mod-8 sum condition)",
                   t_principal, sum_p % 8,
                   f"Sum of coords mod 8 (need 0 for principal coset, 4 for odd)"))

    in_principal = leech2.in_leech(t_principal)
    trace.append(("in_leech", "Lambda_24 (full Construction-C test)",
                   t_principal, in_principal,
                   "Principal-coset candidate membership"))

    # Odd-coset candidate: scale by 4, add canonical glue (-3, +1^23)
    # NOTE: For odd parity (n_ones odd), sum(target) = 4*n_ones + sum(glue).
    # With glue = (-3, +1^23), sum(glue) = 20. For odd parity 4*n_ones ≡ 4 mod 8,
    # so sum(target) ≡ 4 + 20 = 24 ≡ 0 mod 8 -- WRONG coset (needs 4 mod 8).
    # For even parity, sum(target) ≡ 0 + 20 = 20 ≡ 4 mod 8 -- CORRECT (odd coset).
    glue_canonical = (-3,) + (1,) * 23
    t_odd = tuple(4 * ((target_bits >> (23 - i)) & 1) + g for i, g in enumerate(glue_canonical))
    sum_o = sum(t_odd)
    trace.append(("sum_mod_8", "G_24 (mod-8 sum condition for odd coset)",
                   t_odd, sum_o % 8,
                   f"Odd-coset candidate sum mod 8 (need 4)"))
    in_odd = leech2.in_leech(t_odd)
    trace.append(("in_leech", "Lambda_24 (full Construction-C test, odd coset)",
                   t_odd, in_odd,
                   "Odd-coset candidate membership"))

    # Parity wall: if odd, neither candidate works
    wall = None
    if parity == "odd" and not in_principal and not in_odd:
        wall = ("parity_wall: odd-parity binary targets cannot be encoded as "
                "Leech lattice points via 4*binary + canonical glue. The substrate "
                "must escalate to a higher lattice (Barnes-Wall Lambda_32 or "
                "ternary Lambda_48) or use a non-trivial Golay-codeword lift.")

    # Hamming distance to nearest codeword (rule invocation for diagnostic)
    dec = golay_decode.decode_complete(target_bits)
    nearest_cw = dec.corrected if dec.corrected is not None else (
        min(dec.candidates, key=lambda c: (linalg.popcount(c), c))
        if dec.candidates else None)
    if nearest_cw is not None:
        d_h = STEP_RULES["hamming"](target_bits, nearest_cw)
        trace.append(("hamming", "Hamming", (target_bits, nearest_cw), d_h,
                       "Hamming distance from target to nearest Golay codeword"))
        # octad check: if nearest_cw is an octad (weight 8), invoke octads rule
        cw_w = STEP_RULES["popcount"](nearest_cw)
        if cw_w == 8:
            trace.append(("octads", "G_24", nearest_cw, "octad",
                           "The nearest codeword IS an octad (weight-8 codeword)"))
        elif cw_w == 0:
            trace.append(("octads", "G_24", nearest_cw, "zero codeword",
                           "Target is itself a codeword (zero syndrome)"))
        else:
            trace.append(("octads", "G_24", nearest_cw, f"codeword weight {cw_w}",
                           "Nearest codeword is not an octad; weight != 8"))

    return {
        "target_bits": target_bits,
        "n_ones": n_ones,
        "parity": parity,
        "principal_candidate": t_principal,
        "in_principal": in_principal,
        "odd_candidate": t_odd,
        "in_odd": in_odd,
        "wall": wall,
        "trace": trace,
    }


# ============================================================================
# Run on all binary task families
# ============================================================================
def grid_to_bits(grid: np.ndarray) -> int:
    bits = 0
    for v in grid.flatten():
        bits = (bits << 1) | int(v & 1)
    return bits


def measure_task(name: str, n_samples: int = 50) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    fam = TASKS[name]
    snap_results = []
    rule_invocation_counts: Dict[str, int] = {r: 0 for r in STEP_RULES}
    parity_count = {"even": 0, "odd": 0}
    wall_count = 0
    for s in range(n_samples):
        x, _, _ = fam.generate(seed=s + 1, H=4, W=6, dx=1, dy=1)
        bits = grid_to_bits(x)
        snap = snap_to_leech_with_trace(bits)
        snap_results.append({
            "seed": s + 1, "bits": bits, "n_ones": snap["n_ones"],
            "parity": snap["parity"],
            "in_principal": snap["in_principal"],
            "in_odd": snap["in_odd"],
            "wall": snap["wall"] is not None,
        })
        parity_count[snap["parity"]] += 1
        if snap["wall"]:
            wall_count += 1
        # Count rule invocations
        for entry in snap["trace"]:
            rule_name = entry[0]
            if rule_name in rule_invocation_counts:
                rule_invocation_counts[rule_name] += 1
    return {
        "task": name,
        "n_samples": n_samples,
        "parity_distribution": parity_count,
        "wall_count": wall_count,
        "wall_rate": wall_count / n_samples,
        "in_principal_rate": sum(1 for r in snap_results if r["in_principal"]) / n_samples,
        "in_odd_rate": sum(1 for r in snap_results if r["in_odd"]) / n_samples,
        "rule_invocation_counts": rule_invocation_counts,
        "snap_results": snap_results,
    }


def render(results: Dict[str, Any], sample_traces: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    lines = []
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("ITERATION 3 — Step-rule library + Construction-C Leech snap")
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("The 6 step rules (one per substrate layer):")
    for r in STEP_RULES.values():
        lines.append(f"  {r.describe()}")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append("[Per-task snap results — 50 samples each]")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append(f"{'Task':<14}{'even parity':<14}{'odd parity':<14}{'wall rate':<14}"
                 f"{'in principal':<14}{'in odd':<14}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    for name, r in results.items():
        p = r["parity_distribution"]
        lines.append(f"{name:<14}"
                     f"{p['even']:<14}{p['odd']:<14}"
                     f"{100*r['wall_rate']:<14.1f}"
                     f"{100*r['in_principal_rate']:<14.1f}"
                     f"{100*r['in_odd_rate']:<14.1f}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("[Rule invocation counts per task (summed across 50 samples)]")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append(f"{'Task':<14}{'popcount':<12}{'hamming':<12}{'octads':<10}{'sum_mod_8':<12}{'in_leech':<12}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    for name, r in results.items():
        c = r["rule_invocation_counts"]
        lines.append(f"{name:<14}"
                     f"{c.get('popcount',0):<12}"
                     f"{c.get('hamming',0):<12}"
                     f"{c.get('octads',0):<10}"
                     f"{c.get('sum_mod_8',0):<12}"
                     f"{c.get('in_leech',0):<12}")
    lines.append("-" * 96)
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("[Sample trace for one target per task — the step rules in action]")
    lines.append("=" * 96)
    for name, snap in sample_traces.items():
        lines.append(f"\n--- {name} (target_bits = {snap['target_bits']:08x}, n_ones = {snap['n_ones']}, "
                     f"parity = {snap['parity']}) ---")
        for i, (rule, layer, inp, out, note) in enumerate(snap["trace"]):
            inp_str = str(inp)[:50] + ("..." if len(str(inp)) > 50 else "")
            out_str = str(out)[:50] + ("..." if len(str(out)) > 50 else "")
            lines.append(f"  step {i+1}: {rule:<14} [{layer:<28}]  in={inp_str:<25}  out={out_str:<15}  -- {note}")
        if snap["wall"]:
            lines.append(f"  WALL: {snap['wall']}")
        else:
            verdict = "IN LEECH (principal)" if snap["in_principal"] else ("IN LEECH (odd coset)" if snap["in_odd"] else "OUTSIDE LEECH")
            lines.append(f"  VERDICT: {verdict}")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def main():
    os.makedirs("/home/z/my-project/data", exist_ok=True)
    print("Iteration 3: step-rule library + Construction-C snap")
    results = {}
    sample_traces = {}
    for name in ["toggle", "translate", "reflect_v", "flood_fill", "bfs_shortest"]:
        print(f"  {name}...", end=" ", flush=True)
        results[name] = measure_task(name, n_samples=50)
        # Pick a representative sample (first seed) for the trace display
        fam = TASKS[name]
        x, _, _ = fam.generate(seed=1, H=4, W=6, dx=1, dy=1)
        bits = grid_to_bits(x)
        sample_traces[name] = snap_to_leech_with_trace(bits)
        p = results[name]["parity_distribution"]
        print(f"even/odd = {p['even']}/{p['odd']}, wall rate = {100*results[name]['wall_rate']:.0f}%")
    txt = render(results, sample_traces)
    with open(OUT_TXT, "w") as f:
        f.write(txt)
    with open(OUT_JSON, "w") as f:
        json.dump({"results": results, "sample_traces": {k: {**v, "trace": [(list(t)[:4] + [t[4]]) for t in v["trace"]]} for k, v in sample_traces.items()}},
                   f, indent=2, default=str)
    print()
    print(txt)
    print(f"\nWrote: {OUT_JSON}")
    print(f"Wrote: {OUT_TXT}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

iter3_02_plots.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Generate plots for iteration 3."""

import os, json
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.font_manager as fm
try:
    fm.fontManager.addfont('/usr/share/fonts/truetype/chinese/NotoSansSC[wght].ttf')
except Exception:
    pass
try:
    fm.fontManager.addfont('/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSans.ttf')
except Exception:
    pass
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
plt.rcParams['font.sans-serif'] = ['Noto Sans SC', 'DejaVu Sans']
plt.rcParams['axes.unicode_minus'] = False
import numpy as np

DATA = "/home/z/my-project/data"
OUT  = "/home/z/my-project/download/iter3_plots"
os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)


def plot_step_rule_chain():
    """Visualise the 6 step rules across the chain layers."""
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(13, 5), constrained_layout=True)
    ax.axis("off")
    layers = [
        ("F_2",        "popcount",     "count 1-bits",                "#34495e"),
        ("Hamming",    "d_H",          "XOR + popcount",              "#16a085"),
        ("G_23",       "C(23,k) sum",  "sphere-packing identity",     "#27ae60"),
        ("G_24",       "octads",       "759 octads (Steiner S(5,8,24))", "#2980b9"),
        ("Lambda_24",  "theta series", "E_4^3 - 720*Delta",           "#8e44ad"),
        ("Co_0",       "pair census",  "98,280 type-2 axes",          "#c0392b"),
    ]
    x = np.linspace(0.06, 0.94, len(layers))
    for i, (layer, rule, what, color) in enumerate(layers):
        ax.text(x[i], 0.65, layer, ha="center", va="center", fontsize=11,
                bbox=dict(boxstyle="round,pad=0.5", facecolor=color, edgecolor="black"),
                color="white", fontweight="bold")
        ax.text(x[i], 0.45, rule, ha="center", va="center", fontsize=10,
                color=color, fontweight="bold")
        ax.text(x[i], 0.32, what, ha="center", va="center", fontsize=8.5,
                color="#34495e", style="italic")
        if i < len(layers) - 1:
            ax.annotate("", xy=(x[i+1] - 0.04, 0.65), xytext=(x[i] + 0.04, 0.65),
                        arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle="->", lw=2, color="#7f8c8d"))
    ax.text(0.5, 0.92, "The 6 step rules: one per layer of the F_2 -> Hamming -> G_23 -> G_24 -> Lambda_24 -> Co_0 chain",
            ha="center", va="top", fontsize=12, fontweight="bold")
    ax.text(0.5, 0.08, "These are the GLM's computational primitives. Construction-C Leech snap is a composition of these rules.",
            ha="center", va="bottom", fontsize=10, color="#27ae60")
    ax.set_xlim(0, 1); ax.set_ylim(0, 1)
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/step_rule_chain.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/step_rule_chain.png")


def plot_parity_wall():
    """Per-task parity distribution + wall rate."""
    with open(f"{DATA}/iter3_step_rules.json") as f:
        d = json.load(f)
    results = d["results"]
    tasks = list(results.keys())
    even = [results[t]["parity_distribution"]["even"] for t in tasks]
    odd  = [results[t]["parity_distribution"]["odd"]  for t in tasks]
    wall = [results[t]["wall_count"] for t in tasks]
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(11, 5), constrained_layout=True)
    x = np.arange(len(tasks))
    w = 0.35
    ax.bar(x - w/2, even, w, label="even parity (snaps to Leech)",
           color="#27ae60", edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.bar(x + w/2, wall, w, label="parity wall (odd parity)",
           color="#c0392b", edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.set_xticks(x); ax.set_xticklabels(tasks, rotation=15)
    ax.set_ylabel("# of samples (out of 50)")
    ax.set_ylim(0, 55)
    ax.set_title("Phase 3: parity wall — odd-parity binary grids cannot be encoded\n"
                  "as Leech lattice points via 4*binary + canonical glue")
    ax.legend(loc="upper right")
    ax.grid(axis="y", linestyle=":", alpha=0.4)
    for xi, e, wd in zip(x, even, wall):
        ax.text(xi - w/2, e + 1, str(e), ha="center", fontsize=9, color="#27ae60")
        ax.text(xi + w/2, wd + 1, str(wd), ha="center", fontsize=9, color="#c0392b")
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/parity_wall.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/parity_wall.png")


def plot_rule_invocations():
    """Per-task rule invocation counts."""
    with open(f"{DATA}/iter3_step_rules.json") as f:
        d = json.load(f)
    results = d["results"]
    tasks = list(results.keys())
    rule_names = ["popcount", "hamming", "octads", "sum_mod_8", "in_leech"]
    rule_labels = ["popcount\n(F_2)", "hamming\n(Hamming)", "octads\n(G_24)",
                    "sum_mod_8\n(G_24)", "in_leech\n(Lambda_24)"]
    colors = ["#34495e", "#16a085", "#2980b9", "#7f8c8d", "#8e44ad"]
    M = np.zeros((len(tasks), len(rule_names)))
    for i, t in enumerate(tasks):
        c = results[t]["rule_invocation_counts"]
        for j, r in enumerate(rule_names):
            M[i, j] = c.get(r, 0)
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(12, 5), constrained_layout=True)
    x = np.arange(len(tasks))
    w = 0.15
    for j, (rule, label, color) in enumerate(zip(rule_names, rule_labels, colors)):
        ax.bar(x + (j - 2) * w, M[:, j], w, label=label, color=color,
                edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    ax.set_xticks(x); ax.set_xticklabels(tasks, rotation=15)
    ax.set_ylabel("Invocations across 50 samples")
    ax.set_title("Phase 1: step-rule invocations per task family\n"
                  "(Each snap calls popcount, sum_mod_8, in_leech twice; hamming and octads once)")
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", ncols=2, fontsize=8.5)
    ax.grid(axis="y", linestyle=":", alpha=0.4)
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/rule_invocations.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/rule_invocations.png")


def plot_snap_decision_tree():
    """Visualise the Construction-C snap as a decision tree of step rules."""
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(12, 7), constrained_layout=True)
    ax.axis("off")
    # Tree structure
    # Top: input binary target
    # Step 1: popcount(target) -> n_ones
    # Step 2: parity check (n_ones mod 2)
    # Branch A (even): scale by 4 -> principal candidate -> sum_mod_8 check -> in_leech?
    # Branch B (odd): scale by 4 + glue -> odd candidate -> sum_mod_8 check -> in_leech?
    # If both branches fail (odd parity case): WALL -> escalate to Lambda_32 / Lambda_48
    # If one succeeds: VERDICT (in Leech)
    # Diagnostic: hamming to nearest codeword, octad check
    nodes = [
        # (x, y, label, color)
        (0.5, 0.92, "INPUT: binary target (24-bit word)", "#34495e"),
        (0.5, 0.80, "popcount(target) → n_ones  [F_2 rule]", "#34495e"),
        (0.5, 0.68, "parity = n_ones mod 2", "#34495e"),
        (0.25, 0.56, "EVEN: 4*target → principal candidate", "#27ae60"),
        (0.75, 0.56, "ODD: 4*target + glue → odd candidate", "#c0392b"),
        (0.25, 0.44, "sum_mod_8 (need 0)", "#16a085"),
        (0.75, 0.44, "sum_mod_8 (need 4)", "#16a085"),
        (0.25, 0.32, "in_leech test  [Lambda_24 rule]", "#8e44ad"),
        (0.75, 0.32, "in_leech test  [Lambda_24 rule]", "#8e44ad"),
        (0.25, 0.20, "VERDICT: IN LEECH", "#27ae60"),
        (0.75, 0.20, "WALL: parity wall\n(odd parity → no Leech encoding)", "#c0392b"),
        (0.5, 0.08, "Diagnostic: hamming → nearest codeword; octads check\n[Hamming + G_24 rules]", "#7f8c8d"),
    ]
    for x, y, label, color in nodes:
        ax.text(x, y, label, ha="center", va="center", fontsize=9,
                bbox=dict(boxstyle="round,pad=0.4", facecolor=color, edgecolor="black", alpha=0.85),
                color="white", fontweight="bold")
    # arrows
    arrows = [
        ((0.5, 0.88), (0.5, 0.84)),
        ((0.5, 0.76), (0.5, 0.72)),
        ((0.40, 0.64), (0.25, 0.60)),
        ((0.60, 0.64), (0.75, 0.60)),
        ((0.25, 0.52), (0.25, 0.48)),
        ((0.75, 0.52), (0.75, 0.48)),
        ((0.25, 0.40), (0.25, 0.36)),
        ((0.75, 0.40), (0.75, 0.36)),
        ((0.25, 0.28), (0.25, 0.24)),
        ((0.75, 0.28), (0.75, 0.24)),
        ((0.25, 0.16), (0.5, 0.12)),
        ((0.75, 0.16), (0.5, 0.12)),
    ]
    for (x1, y1), (x2, y2) in arrows:
        ax.annotate("", xy=(x2, y2), xytext=(x1, y1),
                    arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle="->", lw=1.5, color="#7f8c8d"))
    ax.text(0.5, 0.97, "Construction-C Leech snap as a composition of step rules",
            ha="center", va="top", fontsize=13, fontweight="bold")
    ax.set_xlim(0, 1); ax.set_ylim(0, 1)
    fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/snap_decision_tree.png", dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Wrote {OUT}/snap_decision_tree.png")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    plot_step_rule_chain()
    plot_parity_wall()
    plot_rule_invocations()
    plot_snap_decision_tree()
    print("\nAll plots written to", OUT)

iter3_03_report.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Iteration 3 PDF report — step rules as the GLM's learning primitives.
"""

import os, sys
from reportlab.lib import colors
from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import A4
from reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle
from reportlab.lib.units import cm, mm
from reportlab.lib.enums import TA_LEFT, TA_CENTER
from reportlab.pdfbase import pdfmetrics
from reportlab.pdfbase.ttfonts import TTFont
from reportlab.platypus import (
    SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, Image, PageBreak,
    Table, TableStyle, KeepTogether
)

pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('BodySerif',   '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/noto-serif-sc/NotoSerifSC-Regular.ttf'))
pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('BodySerifB', '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/noto-serif-sc/NotoSerifSC-Bold.ttf'))
pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('BodySans',    '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/noto-serif-sc/NotoSerifSC-Medium.ttf'))
pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('Mono',        '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSansMono.ttf'))

PAGE_BG       = colors.HexColor('#f1f0ef')
SECTION_BG    = colors.HexColor('#f1f0ee')
CARD_BG       = colors.HexColor('#ebeae5')
TABLE_STRIPE  = colors.HexColor('#ecebe9')
HEADER_FILL   = colors.HexColor('#4a3a1a')
COVER_BLOCK   = colors.HexColor('#3a2e0a')
BORDER        = colors.HexColor('#c9c5bb')
